# Word (DOCX): feature catalog

Level 2 of the feature catalog. Curated by hand in [docx.yaml](docx.yaml); this page is generated by
`dotnet run --project tools/SpecTools -- features`. Element names link to the format catalog (level 1).

Rendering target: one HTML document per Word document with fixed-size pages, laid out and paginated by our own engine the way Word lays the document out for printing. Paragraph text is measured and broken into lines by our own text engine and emitted as positioned glyph runs; drawings, charts and SmartArt go through the DrawingML renderer shared with PowerPoint. The reference is Word's own PDF export of the document.

## Summary

- 194 features in 29 areas
- Coverage: 2,093 of the 2,093 elements that can occur in a DOCX file (100.0 %) are assigned to a feature

Priority: **P0** needed for typical documents, **P1** common, **P2** occasional, **P3** rare or editor-only.
Difficulty: **1** direct mapping to HTML/CSS/SVG … **5** research project.

<a id="package.opc"></a>

### Package, content types and relationships

`package.opc` · P0 · difficulty 1 · done

**Rendering:** As pptx `package.opc` (done): parts are found through relationships, never by path. Word's main part is the officeDocument relationship of the package; styles, numbering, settings, fonts, notes, comments, headers and footers hang off it.

**Office behaviour:** Shared with pptx: the main part is the package's officeDocument relationship; styles, settings, theme, numbering, font table, notes, headers and footers are found through its relationships, pictures and charts through those of the part that refers to them; nothing is found by its path.

**Elements (5):** [`ct:Default`](../format/namespaces/ct.md#ctdefault), [`ct:Override`](../format/namespaces/ct.md#ctoverride), [`ct:Types`](../format/namespaces/ct.md#cttypes), [`pr:Relationship`](../format/namespaces/pr.md#prrelationship), [`pr:Relationships`](../format/namespaces/pr.md#prrelationships)

<a id="package.mce"></a>

### Markup compatibility and extension lists

`package.mce` · P0 · difficulty 2 · done

mc:AlternateContent, mc:Ignorable and the extLst/ext mechanism every newer feature uses.

**Rendering:** As pptx `package.mce` (done): take the first mc:Choice whose Requires prefixes we implement, else mc:Fallback. Word writes its 2010 drawing objects (wps, wpg, wpc) in mc:Choice with a VML w:pict in mc:Fallback, so implementing the Choice side makes the VML path the exception.

**Office behaviour:** Shared with pptx: every part is read with mc:AlternateContent resolved, the first mc:Choice whose required namespaces the engine implements, else mc:Fallback. Word's 2010 shapes, groups, canvases and drawing extensions (wps, wpg, wpc, wp14) take the Choice, so VML is only read where a document has nothing else; ignorable elements and extension lists the engine does not know are skipped.

<a id="package.encryption"></a>

### Encrypted and write-protected files

`package.encryption` · P2 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** The decryption of pptx `package.encryption` (done, [MS-OFFCRYPTO] agile and standard encryption) applies unchanged: a password-protected document is a compound file with an EncryptedPackage stream. Editing restrictions (w:documentProtection, w:writeProtection) only matter to an editor.

**Office behaviour:** The decryption shared with pptx ([MS-OFFCRYPTO] agile and standard) opens password-protected documents: one Word encrypted converts with its password, without one or with a wrong one the converter throws a PasswordException saying which (Fidelity convert --password). Editing restrictions only matter to an editor.

<a id="document.body"></a>

### Document and body

`document.body` · P0 · difficulty 1 · done

**Rendering:** The body's block content (paragraphs, tables, content controls, custom XML, section properties) flows through the layout engine into pages; the last w:sectPr of the body describes the last section.

**Office behaviour:** The body's paragraphs, tables, content controls and custom XML flow through the layout into pages; each section's w:sectPr in the last paragraph of the section, the body's own w:sectPr for the last section. A damaged document with several w:body elements shows them all, one after the other (docx/external/poi/MultipleBodyBug).

**Elements (2):** [`w:body`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wbody), [`w:document`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wdocument)

**Specification:** §17.2.2 Document Body (p.202); §17.2.3 Document (p.203)

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.36, 2.1.37

<a id="document.background"></a>

### Page background

`document.background` · P2 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** Color, gradient, texture or picture behind every page (w:background with its VML fill). Whether Word's PDF export includes it must be measured before it is drawn.

**Office behaviour:** Word prints the page color whether or not w:displayBackgroundShape is set: w:background's color (theme colors with tint and shade) or the fill of its v:background, behind everything on each page (docx/local/misc-nothing, misc-nobg), as its PDF draws it (measured with Word's own presets, docx/local/bgpreset-*, and docx/local/ bggrad-*). A linear gradient lies over a tile of 1025 × 769 of Word's 0.12 pt dots from the page's corner and turns back at the tile's end: at the angles 0 and 180 and at negative multiples of 45 (Word's presets write 0, -90, -45 and -135) a gradient between both colors spans a tile of 512 × 384 dots, as does one out and back at those diagonals; negative angles turn the gradient round, at any focus but 0, ±50 and 100 % Word applies the focus twice; colors blend as on shapes. Radial gradients repeat a tile of 1025 × 769 dots, Word's "from corner" presets (o:fill gradientCenter) one of 512 × 384. Textures tile at the picture's size; a picture over the page Word prints as tiles of its pixels at 0.12 pt each; a pattern as its 8 × 8 bitmap, a point a bit, white in the fill color and black in color2. Known deviations: Word's PDF draws a gradient between two colors with the method linear or none in steps of half its length, flat between them; a texture of 300 dpi tiles 0.12 pt larger in Word.

**Elements (4):** [`a15:backgroundPr`](../format/namespaces/a15.md#a15backgroundpr), [`v:background`](../format/namespaces/v.md#vbackground), [`w:background`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wbackground), [`w:displayBackgroundShape`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wdisplaybackgroundshape)

**Specification:** §17.2.1 Document Background (p.197); §17.15.1.26 Display Background Objects When Displaying Document (p.1022); Part 4 §19.1.2.2 Document Background (p.341); MS-ODRAWXML §2.12.1.1 backgroundPr (p.151)

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.1791, 2.1.35

<a id="document.altChunk"></a>

### Imported content (altChunk)

`document.altChunk` · P2 · difficulty 4 · done

A part in another format (HTML, RTF, plain text, another DOCX) that Word converts when it opens the document.

**Rendering:** DOCX chunks are merged as WordprocessingML; plain text becomes paragraphs. HTML and RTF chunks need an import of their own; until then their text is shown without formatting.

**Office behaviour:** Chunks are converted as Word converts them when it opens the document (compared with the WordprocessingML Word writes when it saves such a document again): a WordprocessingML package's body in the document's formatting, plain text a paragraph per line in Courier New 10.5 pt without spacing, HTML through an import of our own: headings in bold Times New Roman 24 to 7.5 pt, paragraphs in 12 pt with 14 pt auto spacing unless the page's style sheet gives margins (p{margin:0}), list items with Symbol bullets or numbers in the document's font, tables with a pixel of cell spacing (none with cellspacing="0"), outset frames and cells in Word's two grays, the padding of cellpadding, widths in pixels, cells centered and in the document's own spacing and font, header cells bold and centered (docx/local/altchunk, altchunk-tables); CSS borders (border: 1px solid black) single lines of that width on the table and its cells, collapsed borders without cell spacing, CSS cell widths as cell and column widths (docx/external/tika/testAltChunkHTML). A web archive (MHTML, message/rfc822) gives its HTML part (testAltChunkMHT). RTF through an import of our own: Word reads the RTF into a document of its own and pastes it with the destination's styles, so a paragraph whose style the document has (by name) takes it and keeps as direct formatting only what differs from the RTF's own definition of that style, and a style the document lacks comes along with all its formatting; the RTF's styles start from its defaults (\*\defchp, \*\defpap, else the default font, 12 pt and no spacing), without a stylesheet the paragraphs are in Word's own Normal (Aptos 12 pt, 8 pt after, 1.16 lines); character formatting lasts until \plain or the end of its group, paragraph formatting until \pard; fonts by their charset's code page, Unicode with its fallback; list labels from \listtext and \pntext; fields with their instructions and results; PNG, JPEG, EMF and WMF pictures; tables from their rows' cell boundaries, gaps, paddings, borders, shading and merges (docx/local/altchunk-rtf, altchunk-rtf2: Word's own RTF among them, lines within 0.3 pt). Known deviations: an RTF chunk's nested tables are flattened, its footnotes, comments and drawn shapes left out; after an HTML list Word gives the first cell of a following table no space after; a web archive's pictures are left out.

**Elements (3):** [`w:altChunk`](../format/namespaces/w.md#waltchunk), [`w:altChunkPr`](../format/namespaces/w.md#waltchunkpr), [`w:matchSrc`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wmatchsrc)

**Specification:** §17.17.2.1 Anchor for Imported External Content (p.1308); §17.17.2.2 External Content Import Properties (p.1309); §17.17.2.3 Keep Source Formatting on Import (p.1310)

### Subdocuments (master documents)

`document.subDoc` · P3 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** Collapsed subdocuments show as their link; expanded ones are already part of the body.

<a id="settings.compatibility"></a>

### Compatibility mode and options

`settings.compatibility` · P0 · difficulty 4 · done

w:compatSetting compatibilityMode (11 = Word 2003, 12 = 2007, 14 = 2010, 15 = 2013 and later) and the layout options older documents carry; each switches a layout rule of the engine.

**Rendering:** One switch per option in the layout engine, each measured in Word with a generated document that differs only in that option. Mode 15 comes first: it is what current Word writes.

**Office behaviour:** Word lays a document out in its compatibility mode (w:compatSetting compatibilityMode, 12 without one): before mode 15 tables put the first cell's text at the indent, or center their left line on the margin where no indent is given at all (measured with docx/local/c2-tblpos in modes 11 to 15), and widen percentage widths by the outer cells' margins (table.width); before 14 there are no OpenType features and kerning comes from the kern table, every pair of it (docx/local/legkern-*: Times New Roman, Calibri and Arial pairs of Latin, Cyrillic, Greek, quotation marks and superscripts, in modes 11 and 12 as w:useAnsiKerningPairs alike; run.openType); before 15 the hyphenation zone counts (paragraph.hyphenation). Each layout option was measured alone in Word in modes 15 and 12 (docx/local/compat15-* and compat12-*: 65 options on a document of justified, bordered and spaced paragraphs, lists, tabs, small caps, raised text, a table and Japanese text), and those that changed anything again with what their rules depend on, in modes 15, 14, 12 and 11 (c2- to c7-*); each of them switches its rule in the engine. doNotExpandShiftReturn: justified lines that end with a manual line break are not stretched. doNotUseHTMLParagraphAutoSpacing: the space after a paragraph and the space before the next add up instead of overlapping, and HTML auto spacing takes the paragraphs' own values. doNotLeaveBackslashAlone: a backslash in Japanese text (w:lang w:eastAsia ja) shows as a yen sign unless its font is an East Asian one. balanceSingleByteDoubleByteWidth: spaces beside spaces or East Asian characters are half the font size wide where the run's East Asian font is one, and do not squeeze in justified lines. noExtraLineSpacing: exact lines put their baseline below the font's ascent and line gap, at the line's bottom at most. suppressTopSpacing: the first line on a page has its baseline 9.6 pt below its top where its height is exact, and loses its at-least height less 9.6 pt above its text where it is at least, whatever its font (Arial from 8 to 40 pt, Times New Roman, Calibri). Before Word 2013 only (modes 11 to 14; Word 2013 and later ignore them): mwSmallCaps sets small capitals in the largest Macintosh size (7, 9, 10, 12, 14, 18, 24, 36, 48, 60 or 72 pt) below the text's size, 7 pt at least, in the line of the text's size; noLeading drops the line gap Word adds to the fonts' win metrics (11 pt Arial lines 12.29 pt high); suppressTopSpacingWP makes lines as WordPerfect 5 did: the font size and 2 pt high, fixed- pitch fonts without the 2 pt, the descent below the baseline; noSpaceRaiseLower leaves raised and lowered text out of the line height; splitPgBreakAndParaMark moves the mark after a page break that ends its paragraph to the next page as an empty line, and the floating objects after one that begins it (docx/local/breakmark-*); suppressSpacingAtTopOfPage drops the extra of at-least and grid spacing above the first line on a page; allowSpaceOfSameStyleInTable turns contextual spacing off in table cells; doNotUseIndentAsNumberingTabStop lets the tab after a list number pass the hanging indent for a custom stop after it, before Word 2013 only (docx/local/numtabind-*: modes 14, 12, 11). In all modes: doNotBreakWrappedTables keeps floating tables whole (table.floating). Before Word 2013 noColumnBalance leaves the columns above a continuous section break unbalanced (section.columns). Below Word's 0.12 pt device grid and not modeled: suppressBottomSpacing (exact lines one dot lower), truncateFontHeightsLikeWP6 (glyphs printed one dot smaller, positions within 0.5 %), useWord97LineBreakRules (Latin letters up to 0.15 pt, Japanese lines 0.03 pt higher). Measured and not modeled: useAltKinsokuLineBreakRules breaks two of 23 Japanese test paragraphs elsewhere in mode 12. Without effect on Word's printed output in modes 15 and 12, on the probes' paragraphs, lists, tabs, tables (autofit wider than the page, contextual spacing in cells), Japanese text at 23 widths, two columns, a line grid, footnotes and page tops: alignTablesRowByRow, autoSpaceLikeWord95, autofitToFirstFixedWidthCell, convMailMergeEsc, displayHangulFixedWidth, doNotAutofitConstrainedTables, doNotBreakConstrainedForcedTable, doNotSnapToGridInCell, doNotSuppressIndentation, doNotSuppressParagraphBorders, doNotUseEastAsianBreakRules, doNotVertAlignCellWithSp, doNotVertAlignInTxbx, doNotWrapTextWithPunct, footnoteLayoutLikeWW8, forgetLastTabAlignment, growAutofit, layoutRawTableWidth, layoutTableRowsApart, lineWrapLikeWord6, noTabHangInd, printBodyTextBeforeHeader, printColBlack, selectFldWithFirstOrLastChar, shapeLayoutLikeWW8, showBreaksInFrames, spaceForUL, spacingInWholePoints, subFontBySize, suppressSpBfAfterPgBrk, swapBordersFacingPages, ulTrailSpace, underlineTabInNumList, useAnsiKerningPairs (kerning without w:kern), useFELayout, useNormalStyleForList, usePrinterMetrics, useSingleBorderforContiguousCells, useWord2002TableStyleRules, wpJustification, wpSpaceWidth, wrapTrailSpaces, adjustLineHeightInTable, applyBreakingRules, cachedColBalance, and the compatSettings enableOpenTypeFeatures, overrideTableStyleFontSizeAndJustification and doNotFlipMirrorIndents set to 0 in mode 15. Measured along the way and fixed in the base layout: a paragraph after a table gets HTML auto spacing; contextual spacing compares a table's first paragraph with the paragraph before it and the paragraph after it with the table's last, works in cells (a cell's first paragraph follows the cell before or the end of the row before), and where it drops a space after, the next space before gives only its excess; a table whose style is not based on the default table style has no cell margins of its own; small caps set spaces at the small size and keep the line of the text's size. Known deviation: Word before 2010 measures Aptos spaces 0.26 pt wider at 11 pt (docx/local/c3-lines12).

<a id="settings.web"></a>

### Web page settings and frames

`settings.web` · P3 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** Options for saving as a web page. Frames pages (w:frameset) show their frames' documents side by side only in Web Layout; the printed layout shows the frameset document itself.

**Office behaviour:** Web page options matter only when saving as a web page; a frames page prints the frameset document itself.

<a id="style.definitions"></a>

### Paragraph, character, table and numbering styles

`style.definitions` · P0 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Resolve properties in the order of ISO/IEC 29500 17.7.2: document defaults, table style, numbering, paragraph style, run style, direct formatting, each style through its basedOn chain. Toggle properties (b, i, caps, smallCaps, strike, dstrike, vanish, emboss, imprint, outline, shadow) flip along the style chain instead of overriding.

**Office behaviour:** Formatting resolves as ISO/IEC 29500 17.7.2 says: document defaults, table style (with its conditional formats), numbering, paragraph style, character style and direct formatting, each style through its basedOn chain (cycles cut); toggle properties (b, i, caps, smallCaps, strike, dstrike, vanish, emboss, imprint, outline, shadow) flip along the chain (17.7.3); default paragraph, character and table styles apply where none is named. The default paragraph and table style is the last one marked w:default; without one marked, paragraphs take the paragraph style named Normal (in any case, whatever its id), tables none (docx/local/nodefault-*, tdef-*). Word keeps its own Normal Table: a table style of that name shows no borders, indent or formatting of its own, its cells 5.4 pt margins left and right (docx/local/tn-*). A style without w:styleId has its name for an id, as it is, without w:type it is a paragraph style; styles are named by id only (docx/local/styleid: neither the name without its spaces or in another case nor a name of a style with an id applies; docx/external/poi/60329, by SQL Server Reporting Services). The other elements are for editing and change nothing printed.

<a id="style.defaults"></a>

### Document defaults

`style.defaults` · P0 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** The base of every property chain; Word's own defaults apply where the part leaves a property out.

**Office behaviour:** w:rPrDefault and w:pPrDefault are the base of every chain; where they leave a property out Word's own defaults hold: Times New Roman 10 pt, single spacing, no space between paragraphs (docx/local/defaults- empty, 94.3 %). A document without a styles part gets the defaults of Word's Normal template: Aptos 12 pt, complex scripts in Arial, kerning, standard and contextual ligatures, 8 pt after paragraphs, 1.16 lines (docx/local/defaults-none, 91.7 %); so does each of w:rPrDefault and w:pPrDefault a styles part leaves out, also in an empty w:docDefaults (docx/local/nodefaults-none, -empty, -run, -para). A document whose styles update from the template (w:linkStyles) without one attached takes the template's defaults in the theme's minor fonts and its Normal style, which states nothing; its other styles stay, based on them (docx/local/ lsk-1: Arial 10 pt single in Normal turns to 12 pt at 1.16 lines, a heading keeps 16 pt bold; docx/external/libreoffice/extras-ooxmlexport/tdf61000). Theme fonts without a theme part are Word's own since 2023, Aptos Display and Aptos (lsk-1).

**Elements (3):** [`w:docDefaults`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wdocdefaults), [`w:pPrDefault`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wpprdefault), [`w:rPrDefault`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wrprdefault)

**Specification:** §17.7.5.1 Document Default Paragraph and Run Properties (p.657); §17.7.5.3 Default Paragraph Properties (p.659); §17.7.5.5 Default Run Properties (p.660)

<a id="font.table"></a>

### Font table and substitution

`font.table` · P0 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Fonts a document names but the machine lacks are replaced the way Word does it, using the font table's alternate name, PANOSE, family, pitch and character set; shared with pptx `engine.fonts`.

**Office behaviour:** A family the machine lacks is replaced as Word replaces it: a family known by the name (PostScript and other platforms' names: ArialMT, TimesNewRomanPSMT, Calibri-Bold, Helvetica, Helvetica Neue, Times, Courier, Palatino, Geneva, Lucida Grande, and Windows' font substitutes), then the table's w:altName, then a font for the table's character set (symbol: Symbol, Shift JIS: Yu Gothic, Hangul: Malgun Gothic, GB 2312: Microsoft YaHei, Big5: PMingLiU, Thai: Browallia New; Arabic and Hebrew by the table's family: roman Sakkal Majalla and Times New Roman, modern Tahoma, all others Arial, docx/local/font-sub3), else Cambria for serif fonts (w:family roman) and fonts the table does not describe and Calibri for all others, whatever their PANOSE, signature, pitch or the theme (docx/local/font-sub 94.4 %, font-sub2 98 %, every line within 0.1 pt). Caladea and Carlito stand in where Cambria and Calibri are missing too. A list number's missing complex script font becomes Times New Roman instead (see run.complexScript).

<a id="font.embedded"></a>

### Embedded fonts

`font.embedded` · P2 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** Embedded fonts are obfuscated TrueType parts (ISO/IEC 29500 17.8.1: the font key GUID XORed over the first 32 bytes); decode them and use them for layout and output like installed fonts.

**Office behaviour:** Fonts the font table embeds (w:embedRegular, Bold, Italic, BoldItalic) are decoded when first used: obfuscated fonts have their first 32 bytes XORed with the w:fontKey GUID's bytes read from its end (a Gabriola Word embedded decodes to the installed file); they stand behind the installed fonts as in Word and are embedded into the HTML when the browser lacks them (a renamed embedded family renders as in Word, docx/local/embedded-renamed 97.8 %).

**Elements (7):** [`w:embedBold`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wembedbold), [`w:embedBoldItalic`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wembedbolditalic), [`w:embedItalic`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wembeditalic), [`w:embedRegular`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wembedregular), [`w:embedSystemFonts`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wembedsystemfonts), [`w:embedTrueTypeFonts`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wembedtruetypefonts), [`w:saveSubsetFonts`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wsavesubsetfonts)

<a id="font.theme"></a>

### Theme fonts

`font.theme` · P0 · difficulty 2 · done

rFonts asciiTheme, hAnsiTheme, eastAsiaTheme and cstheme refer to the theme's major and minor fonts.

**Rendering:** Resolve theme font references against a:fontScheme (theme.fonts); w:themeFontLang picks the script-specific entries of the theme for East Asian and complex-script text.

**Office behaviour:** rFonts asciiTheme, hAnsiTheme, eastAsiaTheme and cstheme take the theme's major or minor font of their script. Where the theme's East Asian or complex script font is empty, the theme's a:font for the script of the language w:themeFontLang names (ja-JP: Jpan, zh-CN: Hans, zh-TW: Hant, ko-KR: Hang, ar-SA: Arab, he-IL: Hebr …) is taken; without one, East Asian text keeps the run's Latin font and complex scripts take Times New Roman, both then falling back by glyph; the runs' own languages do not matter (docx/local/tf-de, tf-ja, tf- zh, tf-ko, tf-he, tf-none: the East Asian lines within 0.1 pt).

<a id="run.basic"></a>

### Runs, fonts, size and color

`run.basic` · P0 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** Each run picks one of its four fonts (ascii, hAnsi, eastAsia, cs) per character by Unicode range and the w:hint attribute, then is measured and broken into lines by our text engine (engine.lineLayout).

**Office behaviour:** Widths are the fonts' advances shaped by HarfBuzz at the nominal size (not rounded to the 600 dpi device); GPOS kerning only where w:kern is set and the size reaches its threshold, ligatures and contextual alternates only per w14:ligatures and w14:cntxtAlts (measured with docx/generated/text-fonts: positions within 0.1 pt per line without kerning). Theme fonts and theme colors resolve through the theme and the settings' color scheme mapping. Text of automatic color (w:color auto or none) is black, white on dark shading: the run's own, else its paragraph's, else its cell's, where 0.30 red + 0.59 green + 0.11 blue of it is below 60 of 255 (docx/local/autocolor: 100 shadings of each kind, C00000 and 006400 white, C80000, 006600 and 3C3C3C black; highlights, even black, change nothing). Characters the run's font lacks fall back per Unicode range as Word's do (Segoe UI Symbol and Emoji, Cambria Math, Nirmala UI, Leelawadee, MS Mincho, …), the line growing with them, whole clusters together (engine.fonts, docx/local/fallback).

<a id="run.decoration"></a>

### Underline, strike, caps, highlight, borders and shading

`run.decoration` · P0 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** Drawn with the glyph runs as SVG: 18 underline styles with their own color, single and double strike, highlight and shading behind the line box, run borders merged across adjacent runs with the same border.

**Office behaviour:** Measured with docx/local/underlines, ulsizes, ulmixed, supsizes, supsizes2 and raised (Arial, Times New Roman, Calibri, Courier New, Segoe UI, Cambria, Aptos from 8 to 72 pt). Word draws on its 600 dpi device: a single underline from the font's post underline position, as thick as its underline thickness, both in whole 0.12 pt dots; double lines half as thick (rounded up), the first from half the position, the second ending with the single line or 8 dots below the first; thick lines and the heavy patterns fill from the first double line to the second; dotted, dashed, long dashed, dot-dash and dot-dot-dash underlines have fixed lengths whatever the size (1.44/1.44, 3.96/1.92, 7.92/3.96, 3.0/0.96/0.96/0.96, 2.16/0.72 pt) from the underline's start; waves are 2.4 pt long, crests on the page's 2.4 pt grid, 0.24 pt thin (heavy 0.96 pt), within half the double band less 3 dots, wavy double splitting it. Neighbouring runs with the same underline share one, at their lowest baseline, its lines averaging theirs by width; superscripts underline alone at their own baseline; spaces at a line's end stay bare, tabs not; words leaves out spaces. Strikethrough from the OS/2 strikeout position and size, double strikethrough that line moved up and down by its thickness. Emboss: gray (#999999) copy in place, black copy 1.92 pt up-left, the text 0.96 pt up-left; imprint the same down-right; shadow a #C0C0C0 copy a 24th of the size down-right; outline hollow letters stroked 0.14 pt (white over a shadow). Run borders take their lines and space (floored to dots) beside the text in the line, neighbouring runs with one border sharing a frame over the font's height. Super- and subscripts take the font's OS/2 sizes in whole half-points (Calibri 11 pt: 7 pt; Aptos 12 pt: 7 pt), raised by its superscript offset or as far as the line's top allows, lowered by its subscript offset or as far as its bottom allows, in half-points, and make no line higher (within half a point where the line decides). Small caps are 80 % of the size rounded to half points; highlight and shading (patterns mixed over the fill) cover the line's natural box. Known deviations: double and thick underlines of Arial and Courier New below 12 pt stand up to 3 dots higher; Garamond from 12 pt takes other super- and subscript sizes.

**Elements (13):** [`w:bdr`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wbdr), [`w:caps`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wcaps), [`w:dstrike`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wdstrike), [`w:emboss`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wemboss), [`w:highlight`](../format/namespaces/w.md#whighlight), [`w:imprint`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wimprint), [`w:outline`](../format/namespaces/w.md#woutline), [`w:shadow`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wshadow), [`w:shd`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wshd), [`w:smallCaps`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wsmallcaps), [`w:strike`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wstrike), [`w:u`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wu), [`w:vertAlign`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wvertalign)

<a id="run.spacing"></a>

### Character spacing, scale, kerning and position

`run.spacing` · P1 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** Applied in measurement; kerning from the font's kern/GPOS data above the w:kern threshold size.

**Office behaviour:** Character spacing is added after every cluster, horizontal scale stretches the advances, raised and lowered text adds what it reaches beyond the line outside the line spacing multiple (text 3 pt up and 3 pt down makes a 1.15 line 6 pt higher, not 6.95 pt; docx/generated/character-formats). Fit text (w:fitText): neighboring runs with one id fit together into the width; wider text is scaled horizontally to it, narrower text spreads with equal gaps between its characters, the last ending at the width (docx/local/fittext, fittext2). Known deviation: Word also narrows spread characters, to between (n - 1)/n of their width (very wide fits) and all of it, and quantizes the scale to its 600 dpi font widths; characters here keep their width, each standing within about a point of Word's.

**Elements (5):** [`w:fitText`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wfittext), [`w:kern`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wkern), [`w:position`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wposition), [`w:spacing`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wspacing), [`w:w`](../format/namespaces/w.md#ww)

**Specification:** §17.3.1.33 Spacing Between Lines and Above/Below Paragraph (p.256); §17.3.2.14 Manual Run Width (p.289); §17.3.2.19 Font Kerning (p.293); §17.3.2.24 Vertically Raised or Lowered Text (p.299); §17.3.2.35 Character Spacing Adjustment (p.321); §17.3.2.43 Expanded/Compressed Text (p.331) and 1 more

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.60, 2.1.78, 2.1.83, 2.1.96

<a id="run.hidden"></a>

### Hidden text

`run.hidden` · P1 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** Hidden runs take no space in the printed layout unless Word is measured to print them.

**Office behaviour:** Hidden text (w:vanish, w:specVanish) is left out of Word's PDF export and takes no space; the layout drops it the same way. A paragraph whose mark is hidden runs into the next one, the joined paragraph in the first's formatting (alignment, space before); one whose text is all hidden too is gone; before a table it stays without its space after; a cell's last paragraph stays even when all of it is hidden, the body's goes (docx/local/hiddenmark).

**Elements (3):** [`w:specVanish`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wspecvanish), [`w:vanish`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wvanish), [`w:webHidden`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wwebhidden)

**Specification:** §17.3.2.36 Paragraph Mark Is Always Hidden (p.322); §17.3.2.41 Hidden Text (p.329); §17.3.2.44 Web Hidden Text (p.332)

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.101, 2.1.97

<a id="run.complexScript"></a>

### Right-to-left and complex scripts

`run.complexScript` · P1 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Complex-script runs use their own font, size, bold and italic; reordering and shaping as in pptx `text.rtl` (done), with w:bdo and w:dir as explicit embeddings.

**Office behaviour:** Complex script text takes the w:cs font with w:szCs, w:bCs and w:iCs. Bidirectional levels follow UAX #9 with the paragraph's direction as the base, neutrals of w:rtl and w:dir runs reading right to left and w:bdo forcing its direction; lines are reordered between their tabs, right-to-left pieces reach the browser whole from their right end (shaped, mirrored brackets) and justification widens their spaces. Clusters of several characters (marks, conjuncts, emoji sequences) are drawn whole. In a left-to-right paragraph a right-to-left run is an embedding of its own, reversed as a whole with its neutrals ("abc def!" shows "!abc def"; docx/local/bidi 94.1 %, every line as in Word; fallback-marks 93.3 %). Which characters are complex script follows ECMA-376's two steps as Word takes them: by its block a character is ASCII (Basic Latin and the right-to-left scripts: Hebrew, Arabic, Syriac, Thaana, the Arabic presentation forms), East Asian or high ANSI (all else, Thai and the Indic scripts too, which fall back from the high ANSI font and never take the w:cs font); w:cs and w:rtl then make every character complex script in font, size, bold and italic, Latin letters and punctuation too, but in a w:rtl run (not w:cs) numbers keep the font of their block with complex script size and bold, Arabic-Indic digits always, digits and % $ unless the run's w:lang w:bidi is Hebrew; w:hint="cs" changes nothing (docx/local/rtlslot-a to -p, every line as in Word). A w:rtl or w:cs paragraph mark measures its empty line in its w:cs font. A right-to-left paragraph's list number takes the rules of a w:rtl run but for its left-to-right letters ("1." beside Arabic: "1" Times New Roman, "." Aharoni; "a." "a" in the ASCII font), a left-to-right paragraph's none, whatever the w:rtl of its mark or level, and a number's missing w:cs font becomes Times New Roman (docx/local/rtlnum-a to -o). Hebrew text set in Times New Roman beside Aharoni lines had made every line taller (docx/external/powertools/ HC002-Hebrew-01, HC003-Hebrew-02, HC012-Test-07, HC014-RTL-Table-01 from 2–26 % to 100 % of their lines).

**Elements (7):** [`w:bCs`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wbcs), [`w:bdo`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wbdo), [`w:cs`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wcs), [`w:dir`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wdir), [`w:iCs`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wics), [`w:rtl`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wrtl), [`w:szCs`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wszcs)

**Specification:** §17.3.2.2 Complex Script Bold (p.274); §17.3.2.3 Bidirectional Override (p.275); §17.3.2.7 Use Complex Script Formatting on Run (p.280); §17.3.2.8 Bidirectional Embedding Level (p.281); §17.3.2.17 Complex Script Italics (p.292); §17.3.2.30 Right To Left Text (p.317) and 1 more

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.69, 2.1.70, 2.1.73, 2.1.74, 2.1.81, 2.1.92, 2.1.99

<a id="run.content"></a>

### Breaks, symbols and special characters

`run.content` · P0 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** Page, column and text-wrapping breaks (with clear) go to the layout engine; w:sym characters are drawn from their symbol font (private use area F000-F0FF). w:lastRenderedPageBreak is where Word broke the page when it last saved: not rendered, but a free check of our pagination.

**Office behaviour:** Page and column breaks go to the paginator. One that begins a paragraph comes before it where the rest goes on in the next page or column: the number, the space before (what it has more than the space after above it) and the text (docx/local/leadbreak-a … -k, breakmark2-*; docx/external/libreoffice/extras-ooxmlexport/ wDateValueFormat, tdf153964_topMarginAfterBreak15: 52 of 60 pt after a paragraph with 8 pt after). A column break always takes the paragraph mark along, which then holds a line of the next column or page, with the floating objects anchored to it, also after text; a page break only where text or an inline picture follows or w:splitPgBreakAndParaMark says so, which Word 2013 and later ignore (docx/local/breakmark-*, breakmark3-*, breakmark4-* in modes 11, 14 and 15; tdf153613_anchoredAfterPgBreak, -2, -3; tdf153592_columnBreaks). Where a content control starts before the break, as around a table of contents, the break ends the first line like any other and the space before stays behind (leadbreak-j, -m … -q; tdf154478). In table cells page and column breaks break nothing at all, the text going on in its line, in text boxes they end the line (docx/local/cb-1 to cb-5 in modes 14 and 15; fdo68607, table-pagebreak, tdf163178_columnbreakInTable). Text wrapping breaks (and w:cr) end the line; with w:clear the next line moves below the floating objects of the side it clears (an object on the left half of the column is on the left; docx/local/br-clear 99.8 %). Tabs go to the tab stops; w:sym characters are drawn from their symbol font. A line breaking at a soft hyphen shows a hyphen, which has to fit; a no-break hyphen prints the font's hyphen kept with the next character; date blocks show today's day, weekday, month, month name and year in the run's language, else the regional one (docx/local/run-content 96.6 %). w:lastRenderedPageBreak and w16se:symEx (its fallback character is taken) draw nothing of their own. Line feeds and carriage returns kept in w:t show as spaces (CR LF as one), tabs as tabs (docx/local/wtcontrol; docx/external/libreoffice/extras-uiwriter/tdf134021: table cells ending in a line feed stay one line high). Without xml:space="preserve" on the w:t itself or on w:document the spaces, tabs and line ends at the ends of its text are dropped and tabs inside show as spaces, the spaces inside and no-break spaces kept; xml:space on w:body, the paragraph or the run does not count (docx/local/ws-1 to ws-5; docx/external/libreoffice/extras-ooxmlimport/xml_space, tdf124670).

**Elements (14):** [`w16se:symEx`](../format/namespaces/w16se.md#w16sesymex), [`w:br`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wbr), [`w:cr`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wcr), [`w:dayLong`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wdaylong), [`w:dayShort`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wdayshort), [`w:lastRenderedPageBreak`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wlastrenderedpagebreak), [`w:monthLong`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wmonthlong), [`w:monthShort`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wmonthshort), [`w:noBreakHyphen`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wnobreakhyphen), [`w:softHyphen`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wsofthyphen), [`w:sym`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wsym), [`w:tab`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wtab), [`w:yearLong`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wyearlong), [`w:yearShort`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wyearshort)

<a id="run.ruby"></a>

### Ruby (phonetic guides)

`run.ruby` · P2 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Ruby text set above the base text in its own size, aligned by w:rubyAlign; raises the line height.

**Office behaviour:** Ruby sets its phonetic text w:hpsRaise above its base, centered, left or right over it or spread over it (distributeLetter: the gaps between its letters; distributeSpace: also half a gap at either end); a ruby wider than its base spans its own width with the base centered under it; rightVertical runs it down the base's right, centered on the base; the line makes room for it (docx/local/ruby: the lines' baselines within 0.1 pt).

<a id="run.eastAsian"></a>

### East Asian layout and emphasis marks

`run.eastAsian` · P2 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Combined characters, horizontal-in-vertical text, two lines in one and emphasis marks.

**Office behaviour:** Emphasis marks (w:em) over every character but spaces, measured at 12 pt: a dot (U+FF0E) or comma (U+3001) in the run's font 0.33 em in and 1.01 em above the baseline, a ring (U+02DA, Batang) 0.34 em in and 0.29 em above, a dot below (SimSun) 0.3 em in and 0.38 em below. Two lines in one (w:eastAsianLayout w:combine): the text's halves in half size above one another, between full-size brackets (docx/local/ruby). Text across the line (w:eastAsianLayout w:vert, horizontal in vertical text) is a little line of its own turned a quarter counterclockwise against its line, in any text direction (horizontal text too): along the line it takes the height of a line across it (a vertical line's in horizontal text, a horizontal one's in vertical text), its baseline that line's ascent from where it begins; across the line it is centered on the middle of its font's win ascent and descent and makes the line as thick as it is long; w:vertCompress changes nothing in Word's printout (docx/local/vertical, vertical2, vertical3: Yu Mincho, MS Mincho, Meiryo, 2 to 6 digits, lines within 0.1 to 0.3 pt).

**Elements (2):** [`w:eastAsianLayout`](../format/namespaces/w.md#weastasianlayout), [`w:em`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wem)

**Specification:** §17.3.2.10 East Asian Typography Settings (p.283); §17.3.2.12 Emphasis Mark (p.288)

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.75

<a id="run.textEffects"></a>

### Text effects (Word 2010)

`run.textEffects` · P1 · difficulty 3 · done

Glow, shadow, reflection, outline, gradient fill and 3D formats on text.

**Rendering:** Word's copy of the DrawingML text effects in the w14 namespace; map them onto the shared effect renderer (pptx `text.fillOutline`, `text.effects` and `text.threeD`, done).

**Office behaviour:** w14:glow, w14:shadow and w14:reflection are drawn by the effect filter shared with PowerPoint (sized with the font; text reflects about its baseline), w14:textOutline as a stroke, w14:textFill as a solid, gradient or no fill. The line makes room for the effects outside its spacing: a glow 0.778 of its radius, a shadow 1.046 of its blur plus or minus its vertical offset, both at (size / 78)^0.695, a reflection size × (0.944 end − 0.21) and 0.0566 size per point of its distance (docx/local/text-effects*, Arial 12 to 96 pt, lines within 0.3 pt; docx/generated/shapes-effects 99.1 %). 3D text (w14:props3d with a bevel, depth or contour, w14:scene3d) Word draws as pictures of its lit glyphs; here it is PowerPoint's 3D text (pptx text.threeD) in the run's place, its baseline on the run's, under the run's light rig and always facing the viewer (a perspective camera draws as the front view in Word too); the glyphs' counters are holes the bevel runs into (docx/local/text-3d: bevels, depth with a contour, metal, perspective, 78 %). Known deviations: Word's bevels are smoother at sharp corners and lit somewhat differently; a document draws its first 400 characters of 12 pt (fewer of larger ones) of 3D text as bodies, the rest flat, as each 48 pt character takes 85 to 420 KB of markup (docx/external/libreoffice/writerfilter-dmapper/fdo78333-1-minimized: a page of it ran out of memory).

<a id="run.openType"></a>

### OpenType features

`run.openType` · P2 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** OpenType features of the shaper (HarfBuzz, as in pptx `text.runs`): liga, clig, hlig and dlig by the ligature setting, lnum or onum, pnum or tnum, ss01 to ss20 and calt.

**Office behaviour:** The run's OpenType features go to the shaper and to the browser: liga and clig by w14:ligatures (standard), calt by w14:ligatures (contextual) or w14:cntxtAlts, hlig and dlig, lnum or onum by w14:numForm, pnum or tnum by w14:numSpacing, ss01 to ss20 by w14:stylisticSets (docx/local/opentype: all 63 lines within 0.1 pt, every glyph Word's, Gabriola's contextual swashes included). The browser shapes every character that has a position of its own alone, so words with ligatures or contextual glyphs reach it whole with dx shifts, the other words keep the position of every character. Arabic script takes the font's own kerning, ligatures and contextual forms whatever w:kern and w14:ligatures say (Persian in Traditional Arabic is as wide as shaped with them, with w:kern and without, 445 against 465 pt over 34 words, "تم" in its ligature; the Latin letters of a w:rtl run stay unkerned: docx/local/csfeat-a, -b, -d), and its letters do not join across a bidirectional control, which ends Word's shaping run (Persian words with a right-to-left mark after their prefix, 29 of 30 as wide as shaped apart). The browser, which shapes right-to-left text whole, does the same. docx/external/libreoffice/extras-uiwriter/tdf104649 and powertools/RP/RP051-Arabic then break 95 % of their lines as Word does (65 and 54 % before).

**Elements (6):** [`w14:cntxtAlts`](../format/namespaces/w14.md#w14cntxtalts), [`w14:ligatures`](../format/namespaces/w14.md#w14ligatures), [`w14:numForm`](../format/namespaces/w14.md#w14numform), [`w14:numSpacing`](../format/namespaces/w14.md#w14numspacing), [`w14:styleSet`](../format/namespaces/w14.md#w14styleset), [`w14:stylisticSets`](../format/namespaces/w14.md#w14stylisticsets)

**Specification:** MS-DOCX §2.6.1.2 cntxtAlts (p.38); MS-DOCX §2.6.1.17 ligatures (p.44); MS-DOCX §2.6.1.18 numForm (p.44); MS-DOCX §2.6.1.19 numSpacing (p.45); MS-DOCX §2.6.1.24 stylisticSets (p.46); MS-DOCX §2.6.3.34 CT_StyleSet (p.67)

<a id="paragraph.basic"></a>

### Paragraphs, alignment and indentation

`paragraph.basic` · P0 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Left, right, first-line and hanging indents (mirrored on even pages with w:mirrorIndents); alignment left, center, right, justified, distributed and the Arabic kashida variants.

**Office behaviour:** Justified lines may squeeze the spaces between their words to take one more word: where the spaces shrink less than half as much as the spaces of the line without the word would stretch, and by less than a quarter (docx/local/compress3: 225 lines of random words alike; compress, compress2: a Russian line taken at 23.6 %, not at 25.9 %; jc-compress: ten Courier New words fit a column 12 pt narrower than they are, not 13 pt); left, centered and right aligned lines take no word that does not fit (docx/local/jc-left). The last line of a justified paragraph keeps its natural spacing unless it was squeezed. Mirrored indents (w:mirrorIndents) trade the left and right indent on pages with even numbers, the lines breaking as on odd pages (docx/local/mirror: every line as in Word). Medium and high kashida justification lengthen every Arabic word by two or three kashidas at one joining place, chosen by the Arabic justification priorities (after seen or sad, before a final teh marbuta, heh or dal, before a final alef, tah, lam or kaf, before a medial beh, before a final waw, ain, qaf or feh, before another final letter), and justify with them (docx/local/kashida). Known deviations: low kashida justification widens spaces where Word inserts single kashidas; lines with kashidas break up to a word differently.

**Elements (6):** [`w:ind`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wind), [`w:jc`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wjc), [`w:mirrorIndents`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wmirrorindents), [`w:p`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wp), [`w:pPr`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wppr), [`w:pStyle`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wpstyle)

<a id="paragraph.spacing"></a>

### Line and paragraph spacing

`paragraph.spacing` · P0 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Line spacing auto (multiples of the font's single line height), exact and at least; spacing before and after, auto spacing of HTML paragraphs, contextual spacing between paragraphs of the same style. How Word derives single line height from the font tables is measured, not assumed.

**Office behaviour:** A single line is the font's hhea ascender, descender and line gap; the baseline lies the larger of the line gaps (hhea against OS/2 win, as DirectWrite does; OS/2 typo metrics where USE_TYPO_METRICS is set) plus the ascent below the line's top. Fonts for East Asian code pages (OS/2 ulCodePageRange1 bits 17 to 21) are 1.3 times their win ascent and descent high, the extra half above and half below, their line gap ignored (docx/local/ea-lines: MS Gothic, MS Mincho, Yu Gothic, SimSun, Malgun Gothic, Microsoft YaHei). Auto spacing multiplies the line and puts the extra below the text; below single spacing the line loses its share above the baseline and below it alike (docx/local/linemult-120 to -192, in modes 12, 14 and 15: Arial, Times New Roman, Calibri, Cambria and Traditional Arabic at 0.5 to 0.8 within 0.13 pt; negative values do not count), exact spacing puts the baseline at 80 % of the height whatever the font, at least spacing puts the extra above (measured with 14 fonts, docx/local/lm-*). The space between paragraphs is the larger of the space after the one and before the next (docx/generated/text- basic, as ISO/IEC 29500 17.3.1.33 says); contextual spacing drops both between paragraphs of the same style. HTML auto spacing (14 pt) in table cells lies between a cell's blocks, after a nested table too, none at the cell's top or bottom (docx/local/autospace-cells-15, -14).

**Elements (2):** [`w:contextualSpacing`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wcontextualspacing), [`w:spacing`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wspacing)

**Specification:** §17.3.1.9 Ignore Spacing Above and Below When Using Identical Styles (p.216); §17.3.1.33 Spacing Between Lines and Above/Below Paragraph (p.256); §17.3.2.35 Character Spacing Adjustment (p.321)

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.60, 2.1.96

<a id="paragraph.pagination"></a>

### Keep with next, keep lines together, widow control, page break before

`paragraph.pagination` · P0 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Constraints for the page breaker (engine.pagination).

**Office behaviour:** At the top of a page the space before is kept on the document's first page, after a section break only what it has more than the space after the section's last paragraph, and dropped after page and column breaks, page break before and natural breaks (docx/local/sp-pagetop). The extra space of auto line spacing below the last line may hang past the bottom margin (a line whose text ends 0.4 pt above the margin stays, docx/generated/headers-footers). Keep with next, keep lines together and widow control move whole paragraphs or two lines (docx/generated/breaks). A paragraph that does not fit and cannot break where it stands (a line or two, too few lines for widow control, its lines kept together) takes the paragraphs kept with it before it to the next page, back to one that can break and give its last lines or one that is not kept with the next; reaching the top of the page, they stay and the chain breaks where it must. So a chain begun below other text moves to the next page even when it is taller than a page, and lines kept together move although they need more than a page (docx/local/kc1 … kc12). The columns of a section that begins below other text count as below other text: a chain that does not fit moves on column by column, to the next page (docx/local/kcs-2-*, kcs-3-*). A first line that ends in a page or column break needs no second line with it, a paragraph that ends with one ends its chain (docx/external/libreoffice/ extras-ooxmlexport/testPageref: a page break kept with the next paragraph through its style makes no empty page); an empty paragraph that holds a continuous section break and takes no room ends a chain too, as one that always fits (docx/local/keepsect-k1 to -k4: a heading kept with it stays at the bottom of the page, with "x" in the paragraph or without the break it goes on), and a line of nothing but such a break stays on its page, past the bottom too (docx/external/libreoffice/extras-ooxmlexport/FDO77715: no empty pages before headings that begin with a column break). Tables take part: a row whose first cell's first paragraph is kept with the next one carries the chain on to the next row or block and does not break for it, its other cells and paragraphs do not count; the first row of the chain that is not kept needs only to begin on the page, all of it where it may not split (or has a height at least that does not fit), so a paragraph kept with a table goes along with a first row that cannot begin below it. A chain that begins at a row inside a table moves to the next column or page the same way (docx/local/tk1 … tk13, tkr-a … tkr-h; docx/external/libreoffice/extras-ooxmlexport/ tdf104061_tableSectionColumns: a heading row goes on with a row of 173 pt at least).

**Elements (4):** [`w:keepLines`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wkeeplines), [`w:keepNext`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wkeepnext), [`w:pageBreakBefore`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wpagebreakbefore), [`w:widowControl`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wwidowcontrol)

**Specification:** §17.3.1.14 Keep All Lines On One Page (p.235); §17.3.1.15 Keep Paragraph With Next Paragraph (p.236); §17.3.1.23 Start Paragraph on Next Page (p.247); §17.3.1.44 Allow First/Last Line to Display on a Separate Page (p.271)

<a id="paragraph.borders"></a>

### Paragraph borders and shading

`paragraph.borders` · P1 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Consecutive paragraphs with identical borders share one box with w:between lines; borders sit outside the text by their w:space. Shading fills the box, patterns included.

**Office behaviour:** Paragraphs with the same borders and indents form one box, whatever their shading (docx/local/bordergroup-*; without borders the same shading): the top line above the first, the bottom line below the last, the between line between them with its space above and below it; each line and its space take room in the paragraph, the space before and after lie outside the box. The shading reaches 1.4 pt left and 1.55 pt right of the text (vertically just its lines), the side lines stand their space outside it (measured with docx/local/pbdr, all lines within 0.15 pt). Double and thin-and-thick lines are drawn in their strokes as table borders are (a thin-thick top line 3 pt above 0.75 pt, docx/external/poi/FancyFoot); headers and footers draw their paragraphs' borders and shading too, and table cells and text boxes, where the lines take room alike (docx/external/libreoffice/extras-layout/fdo48718-1: bordered paragraphs in a cell, the rows below 3 pt lower each).

<a id="paragraph.tabs"></a>

### Tab stops

`paragraph.tabs` · P0 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Left, center, right, decimal and bar tabs with leaders, cleared style tabs, default tab interval; absolute position tabs (w:ptab) relative to the margins or indents.

**Office behaviour:** Leaders repeat their character at its advance rounded to Word's 600 dpi device (dots of Aptos 12 pt every 3.48 pt), on a grid from the page's left edge, every one that fits between the tab's start and end. Bar tabs draw a line through the line and the space after the paragraph. Decimal stops align on the settings' decimal symbol (with a German comma, 12.5 ends at the stop). A custom stop at or past the right indent holds: from Word 2013 on where it is not past the column's or the cell's edge, the rest of the line then reaching up to that edge and justified to it where it wraps (not where it ends in a line break, docx/local/jz-a to jz-d), the line not breaking next to such tabs (a word that does not fit after them breaks inside where it has no other place); before Word 2013 also past the edge, the line then reaching 22 in into the column (docx/local/tov-a to tov-i, tovc-1 to tovc-12, tv-a to tv-d in modes 15, 14 and 12; docx/external/libreoffice/extras-ooxmlexport/tdf77417, extras-ooxmlimport/ tdf156078_rightTabOutsideParaRightIndent). Default stops past the indent do not hold, except on a line an earlier stop took further. From Word 2013 on a left stop past the column's edge moves the tab to the next line, where it fills the line; centered, right and decimal stops past it align at the indent, or at the edge where an earlier stop took the line there (docx/generated/tabs, docx/local/tab-rules, tab-break). Centered and decimal text that would reach past the line's end ends there (tv-c, tv-d). A tab goes to the next line together with the word after it where that word does not fit after it (docx/local/lw-6 to lw-10; engine.lineLayout). An absolute position tab (w:ptab) aligns what follows it at the left edge, the middle or the right edge of the margins or indents, as a stop of that alignment there would (docx/external/poi/ThreeColHead, ThreeColFoot: within 0.1 pt).

**Elements (5):** [`w:decimalSymbol`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wdecimalsymbol), [`w:defaultTabStop`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wdefaulttabstop), [`w:ptab`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wptab), [`w:tab`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wtab), [`w:tabs`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wtabs)

**Specification:** §17.3.1.37 Custom Tab Stop (p.263); §17.3.1.38 Set of Custom Tab Stops (p.265); §17.3.3.23 Absolute Position Tab Character (p.357); §17.3.3.32 Tab Character (p.366); §17.15.1.23 Radix Point for Field Code Evaluation (p.1018); §17.15.1.25 Distance Between Automatic Tab Stops (p.1021)

<a id="paragraph.frames"></a>

### Text frames and drop caps

`paragraph.frames` · P2 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Paragraphs with w:framePr are positioned like floating objects and text wraps around them; drop caps are frames of one large letter spanning w:lines lines.

**Office behaviour:** Consecutive paragraphs with the same w:framePr form a frame that takes no room in the text: w:w wide or as wide as its content, at w:x or aligned (w:xAlign) in its column, the margin or the page, w:y below the next paragraph's top, the margin or the page, its first paragraph's space before inside it above its lines (docx/local/fb-*: 6 and 12 pt before take its text and the text below it as much lower; fdo78904_fileHang); the paragraphs' borders frame it, all the way down where w:h makes it higher than its content, its last paragraph's space after inside them (docx/external/libreoffice/extras-ooxmlexport/tdf131420, tdf133702, tdf133924: within 0.1 pt); text wraps around it with its borders, w:hSpace and w:vSpace kept free (not beside it with notBeside, over it with none). A drop cap is a frame of its letter at the start of the next paragraph, whose lines go around it; a margin drop cap stands left of the column (docx/local/frames: every line as in Word). Frames in headers and footers stand alike, taking no room in their story (docx/external/libreoffice/extras-ooxmlexport/tdf168017: a page number framed centered 1 twip below the next paragraph's top stands on that paragraph's line). A paragraph's w:framePr overrides its style's attribute by attribute (special_styles), w:x and w:xAlign as one position and w:y and w:yAlign as another (docx/local/framepos: a paragraph's w:x of the page, 0 too, over its style's xAlign right of the margin; tdf112446_frameStyle), one without any w: attribute makes no frame (docx/local/fe-5, fg-7; tdf133457). w:y and w:yAlign count from the margin without w:vAnchor (fd-1, fd-3, fd-5); a frame without a vertical position (w:y 0, no w:yAlign or inline) stands at its paragraph before mode 15 whatever its anchor and on its anchor's top from mode 15 (ff-*, fg-*, fh-*; core-ooxml-pass/fdo81491). The body's text goes around frames standing on the page, also lines above them and around those of headers (special_styles: an envelope address at the page's bottom; fh-1). Lines above a frame standing with the text that it reaches with its w:vSpace go below it (notBeside), the frame keeping its place, and a next frame follows them (fi-2, fi-3, fj-2; tdf105035_framePrB: a title and an author line framed with vSpace 187; tdf164500_framePrBeforeTable); the last paragraph's space after stands in the frame (fk-*).

**Elements (2):** [`w:framePr`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wframepr), [`w:suppressOverlap`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wsuppressoverlap)

**Specification:** §17.3.1.11 Text Frame Properties (p.218); §17.3.1.36 Prevent Text Frames From Overlapping (p.263)

<a id="paragraph.eastAsian"></a>

### East Asian line breaking and character grid

`paragraph.eastAsian` · P1 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Kinsoku (characters that may not start or end a line, custom lists in settings), hanging punctuation, automatic spacing between Asian and Latin text and digits, punctuation compression, and snapping to the section's document grid (section.docGrid).

**Office behaviour:** Kinsoku (w:kinsoku) with Word's characters per East Asian language of the paragraph's first East Asian character, measured with docx/local/kinsoku-chars* (154 candidates each where it would begin and where it would end a line): Japanese standard !%),.:;?]}¢°’”‰′″℃、。々〉》」』】〕゛゜ゝゞ・ヽヾ！％），．：；？］｝｡｣､･ﾞﾟ￠ may not begin a line and $([\{£¥‘“〈《「『【〔＄（［｛｢￡￥ not end one, w:strictFirstAndLastChars adds the small kana and ー; Simplified and Traditional Chinese have their own lists, Korean none, other languages take the Japanese ones. A document's own lists (w:noLineBreaksBefore, w:noLineBreaksAfter for a w:lang) replace both of Word's, a missing one by none (kinsoku-custom*); Word looks them up as written in code point order, as it writes them. East Asian punctuation and full-width forms break like ideographs (a line may end between 。 and 「, ea- adjust). Punctuation that may not begin a line hangs past the end instead where w:overflowPunct lets it (on by default): before Word 2013 in every paragraph, from then on in justified ones; Japanese ,.’”、。」』】），．］｝｡､, Chinese more (kinsoku-chars12-*, -both, -distribute); the line before it fills its width. Only paragraphs with East Asian text or an East Asian language for it hang punctuation: a comma after Latin words goes down with its word (hangcomma-*; docx/external/poi/bug65649), before Word 2013 with ja-JP as the East Asian language it hangs (c3-lines12-*; docx/external/libreoffice/core-ooxml-pass/fdo63164-1), from Word 2013 on the language alone does not do (hangcomma-h4, -h7; extras-ooxmlexport/tdf163894). w:wordWrap off breaks Latin words anywhere (ea-opts). w:topLinePunct sets an East Asian opening bracket that begins a line half its width further left (toplinepunct15/12). In kerned runs (w:kern) an East Asian comma, period or closing bracket before another one or an opening bracket loses half its width unless w:noPunctuationKerning (ea-punctkern). The automatic space between East Asian and Latin text (w:autoSpaceDE, w:autoSpaceDN, on by default) exists only where the document's default East Asian font (w:docDefaults, through the theme) is an East Asian one (ea-auto-*: MS Mincho, SimSun, Yu Gothic yes whatever the language; Arial, none, a style's or the paragraph mark's no): a quarter of the East Asian character's size after East Asian text, half the Latin font's average character width (OS/2) after Latin text (ea-autospace2: Arial, Times New Roman, Calibri, Courier New, Segoe UI beside four East Asian fonts within 0.1 pt), none at a line's end. Justified lines with East Asian text (where that default font is one) share their free width equally among the gaps after and before every East Asian character and after every space, a space 0.45 of its width more and the automatic space at an East Asian/Latin boundary once more; distributed lines among all characters where the paragraph has East Asian text or the document such a font, else among their spaces (cjk-justify-lines, cjk- justify15/12, cjk-justify-western, docx/generated/paragraph-alignment). Before Word 2013 justified lines do not squeeze their spaces for one more word. East Asian fonts are their win ascent and descent high and 15 % of that more above and below, each rounded to the font's units (ea-pitch: nine fonts at 10.5, 12 and 16 pt within 0.003 em). w:textAlignment top, center and bottom align a line's characters by their fonts' win ascents and descents (ec-none); punctuation compression (w:characterSpacingControl) and w:adjustRightInd changed nothing in Word's printout (ec-*, ea-adjust). Known deviation: custom kinsoku lists not in code point order lose different characters in Word than here.

<a id="paragraph.bidi"></a>

### Right-to-left paragraphs and text direction

`paragraph.bidi` · P1 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Right-to-left paragraphs mirror indents and alignment; vertical text direction in frames and cells.

**Office behaviour:** A right-to-left paragraph (w:bidi) is laid out from its start edge and mirrored: w:left and w:start indent the right side, left and start alignment align right, tab stops count from the right margin and trailing spaces hang past the left one (docx/local/bidi: single and justified lines, indents, first line indent, tabs, mixed text, numbers and brackets as in Word). Vertical text (w:textDirection) of a section is laid out on the page turned a quarter, its header, footer and page border upright (docx/local/vertical, vertical2): tbRl runs the lines down from the right margin with East Asian characters upright, btLr the same with all text turned, tbRlV and tbLrV from the left margin with East Asian characters upright, lrTbV keeps lines across and lays East Asian characters on their side; a section with another direction begins a new page. Upright characters are turned a quarter counterclockwise against their line about the middle of their em box (half an em above the BASE table's ideographic baseline, else the middle of the win metrics) in their vertical forms (OpenType vert). Vertical lines are 1/32 of their size thicker than horizontal ones (1.3 times that for East Asian fonts), half above and half below the text (docx/local/vertical3, vertical4: Yu Mincho, MS Mincho, Meiryo, Yu Gothic and Arial at 8 to 28 pt, single and 1.5 lines; exact and at least spacing keep their heights); lines that stack from the left keep the extra of their spacing on the side the next line follows. Table cells are table.cells, text boxes drawing.textBoxes, frames paragraph.frames. Known deviation: floating drawings on pages of vertical text stand upright where their middles turn to.

**Elements (2):** [`w:bidi`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wbidi), [`w:textDirection`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wtextdirection)

**Specification:** §17.3.1.6 Right to Left Paragraph Layout (p.209); §17.3.1.41 Paragraph Text Flow Direction (p.268); §17.4.72 Table Cell Text Flow Direction (p.477); §17.6.1 Right to Left Section Layout (p.557); §17.6.20 Text Flow Direction (p.612)

<a id="paragraph.hyphenation"></a>

### Hyphenation

`paragraph.hyphenation` · P1 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Automatic hyphenation per language with our own pattern-based hyphenator; which word breaks Word chooses with its proofing dictionaries is measured on generated documents. Soft and non-breaking hyphens are run content (run.content).

**Office behaviour:** Automatic hyphenation (w:autoHyphenation) by Liang's algorithm with the hyph-utf8 patterns of German (reformed spelling) and American and British English, by the language of each word (w:lang, else the document's culture), at least 2 letters before and 2 (German) or 3 (English) after a break; w:suppressAutoHyphens turns it off for a paragraph, w:doNotHyphenateCaps for words in capitals, w:consecutiveHyphenLimit limits the lines in a row that end with a hyphen. As Word breaks (measured with docx/local/hyph-a to -d and hyph-fit, every line as in Word): the word that does not fit breaks at its last place where its part and a hyphen fit; justified lines may squeeze their spaces by 23 % for it (Word takes 21.9 %, not 25.2 %); other lines break so only where they would end more than the hyphenation zone short: 0.25 in from Word 2013 on whatever w:hyphenationZone says (zones of 0, 0.25 and 1 in break alike), w:hyphenationZone in older compatibility modes; a word longer than a line breaks at its last fitting place; a line ending with a hyphen does not end a column or page. Known deviations: Word's own dictionaries may allow other places than the patterns (it leaves un-characteristically whole); other languages are not hyphenated.

**Elements (5):** [`w:autoHyphenation`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wautohyphenation), [`w:consecutiveHyphenLimit`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wconsecutivehyphenlimit), [`w:doNotHyphenateCaps`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wdonothyphenatecaps), [`w:hyphenationZone`](../format/namespaces/w.md#whyphenationzone), [`w:suppressAutoHyphens`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wsuppressautohyphens)

<a id="paragraph.htmlDivs"></a>

### HTML divisions

`paragraph.htmlDivs` · P2 · difficulty 3 · done

Paragraphs of documents that came from HTML refer to nested divisions with margins and borders.

**Rendering:** Division margins add to the paragraph indents and spacing; division borders are drawn around their paragraphs.

**Office behaviour:** Paragraphs of HTML divisions (w:divId) take the margins of their division and the divisions around it as indents; bordered divisions frame their paragraphs like paragraph borders at the division's indents, their top and bottom lines with their space before the first and after the last paragraph. HTML auto spacing (w:beforeAutospacing, w:afterAutospacing) is 14 pt, none before the document's first paragraph or after a table (docx/local/html-divs, a page Word converted from HTML: 97.5 %, every line as in Word).

<a id="numbering.definitions"></a>

### Numbering definitions and number formats

`numbering.definitions` · P0 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Level text with %1..%9 placeholders, about 60 number formats (decimal, roman, letters, ordinal and cardinal text, East Asian, Hebrew, Arabic, Thai, Hindi ...), legal numbering, suffix tab, space or nothing, number alignment, the level's run and paragraph properties.

**Office behaviour:** Level texts with %1 to %9, about 60 number formats (decimal, roman, letters repeated after z, ordinals and number words, full-width, enclosed, Chinese, Japanese and Korean counting, Hebrew, Arabic, Thai, Hindi, Russian), legal numbering, suffix tab, space or nothing, numbers aligned left, centered or right at the first line's indent (docx/local/num-rules). Number words and ordinals follow the paragraph's language (English, German, French, Russian; words only below a million); letters repeat up to thirty times, then start over; Chinese counting goes digit by digit from 100 (○ for zero); the heavenly stems turn decimal after ten; Word 2010's custom formats pad with zeros (docx/external/powertools/LIR001 … LIR020 and docx/local/numwords2-*: every number exactly as Word writes it). The tab after a number takes the left indent as its stop also where the number ends right at it (an empty number, one right-aligned at the indent), leaving the text there; custom and default stops count only after its place, as for other tabs (docx/local/emptylabel, tabzero, tabzero2; docx/external/libreoffice/extras-ooxmlexport/tdf153042_largeTab). The level's indents and tabs override the paragraph style's (a level indented 360 twips puts a List Paragraph, whose style says 720, at 360; docx/generated/lists); where the numbering comes from the paragraph style itself, the style's override the level's attribute by attribute (a style's left 1080 with a level's left 1620 and hanging 360 makes left 1080, hanging 360; docx/local/styleind-A … -H). The number is drawn in the paragraph mark's formatting with the level's run properties over it; only its ascent counts in the line height, not its descent (a 16 pt number before 12 pt text makes the line 17.52 pt high), and beside nothing but drawings the line has no descent at all (docx/local/lt-8, lt-10: a bullet beside a 40 pt shape makes the line 40 pt high). A level without w:start counts from 0, where letters and roman numbers show nothing (docx/local/ns-*: "0.", "1.", "2." in modes 12 to 15, ".", "A.", "B."; docx/external/libreoffice/ extras-ooxmlexport/tdf132754). Picture bullets are numbering.pictureBullets.

<a id="numbering.counting"></a>

### List counters

`numbering.counting` · P0 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Counters run per abstract numbering through the whole document (headers, footers and notes keep their own state), restart by w:lvlRestart, w:startOverride and numbering instances; measured against Word because the specification leaves gaps.

**Office behaviour:** Instances of one abstract definition count together; an instance with a start override restarts the shared counter where it is first used, and the other instances go on from there (5, 6, then 7); a deeper level used before a higher one shows the higher one's start and counts from it (7.1.1. then 7.2.); w:lvlRestart 0 never restarts; paragraphs numbered through their style continue the same counters (docx/local/num-rules). A paragraph of nothing but a section break takes no number and counts for none, in modes 14 and 15 (docx/local/ sn-1 to sn-6: items 1, 2, an empty numbered section end, then 3; docx/external/libreoffice/extras-ooxmlexport/ section_break_numbering, tdf170602_exportContext).

### Picture bullets

`numbering.pictureBullets` · P2 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** The bullet is the picture of w:numPicBullet (VML or DrawingML), sized to the paragraph's font.

**Office behaviour:** A level with w:lvlPicBulletId shows the picture of its w:numPicBullet (VML or DrawingML) where the number would stand, on the baseline, 0.996 × size − 1.97 pt high (from 8 to 48 pt: 6 to 45.85 pt) and as wide as its aspect ratio makes it; the tab after it goes on from its end (picture bullet probes Word made, 94 %).

<a id="section.page"></a>

### Page size, margins and section breaks

`section.page` · P0 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Every section starts on a new page, an even or odd page (inserting a blank page) or continues on the same page; orientation, margins with gutter, mirrored margins and vertical alignment of the page content.

**Office behaviour:** Sections start on a new page, continue, or start on an odd or even page; odd and even mean the page numbers, not the sheets: an odd page section after a page numbered 1 leaves a blank page even when the restart put that page on the second sheet (docx/local/hf-oddpage). With different odd and even headers or mirrored margins any section whose first number has the parity of the page before gets a blank page before it (docx/local/hf-restart-*). A section on a new page right after a page break begins on the page the break began while that holds nothing, an empty paragraph holding the break taking no line there (docx/local/pbs-*: no empty page between, also before a landscape section or after a break that ends a paragraph with text; of two page breaks one page stays empty; docx/external/poi/bug65649). Before Word 2013 a page break in the paragraph that ends a section goes unseen where the next section goes on on the same page (docx/local/pb14*, pb12*: "A", a page break holding a continuous section break and "B" on one page, in mode 15 on two; docx/external/libreoffice/extras-uiwriter/tdf131963), unless w:splitPgBreakAndParaMark moves the mark and the section break after it (pbsp14*, pbsp12*). Blank pages have no header or footer, count in NUMPAGES but not in SECTIONPAGES. Vertical alignment centers the text from the top margin, with the space before its first paragraph, to the space after its last (docx/generated/page-setup, page 4; docx/local/va-1 to va-5 in modes 12 to 15), an empty paragraph that only ends the section left out (vb-1 to vb-3); justified (both) it spreads the room left evenly between the paragraphs (va-6; extras-ooxmlexport/tdf36117_verticalAdjustment). Known deviation: with a frame on the page Word centers otherwise (tdf36117, page 5). Section form protection (w:formProt) only guards editing and shows nothing; pages keep their sizes in the HTML whatever a browser can print. What the document leaves out comes from Word's Normal template, which depends on the region (measured on German Word, docx/local/nosect*): without w:sectPr A4, with one but without w:pgSz Letter; missing margins 2.5 cm, the bottom 2 cm, header and footer 0.5 in; regions measuring in inches get Letter and 1 inch.

**Elements (9):** [`w:formProt`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wformprot), [`w:gutterAtTop`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wgutterattop), [`w:mirrorMargins`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wmirrormargins), [`w:pgMar`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wpgmar), [`w:pgSz`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wpgsz), [`w:rtlGutter`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wrtlgutter), [`w:sectPr`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wsectpr), [`w:type`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wtype), [`w:vAlign`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wvalign)
