<a id="section.columns"></a>

### Columns

`section.columns` · P1 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Equal or individual column widths with separator lines; a continuous section break balances the columns above it.

**Office behaviour:** An empty paragraph holding a section break after other text of its section takes no room before a continuous section (docx/local/colsect-*: no line, no space, in balanced columns too); alone in its section or before a new page it is a line as any other. All columns of a continuous section begin below the space after the paragraph above it; its first paragraph adds what its space before has more (docx/local/colsp-*). A continuous section break balances the columns above it (before Word 2013 not with w:noColumnBalance, docx/local/colsect-h, -i) by height in whole lines: the columns are as high as the least multiple of the section's least line height that holds all lines, each filled as far as that height lets it with widow, keep-with-next and keep-together rules (a height at which they cannot hold is not taken: the last two lines of a paragraph stand together in the first of three columns, docx/local/kcs-3-4); the first column holds the space before its first paragraph, a column's last line needs its text but not the extra space of multiple line spacing below it, nor the space after its paragraph (docx/local/cb-*, cbx-*, cbs-*: ten 13.6 pt lines below 13.5 pt go 5/5, below 13.6 pt 4/6; cbp-*: four paragraphs of four lines with A after move two lines once the first column passes the least multiple of 13.6 pt that holds the second with them, at 27.2 to 57.6 pt before for A = 0 to 24 pt; cbq-*: 1.16 lines in multiples of 15.75 pt; cbr*-*: one 27.2 pt line or lines of 20.4 pt keep the 13.6 pt grid; docx/generated/page-setup: a heading and 60 lines go 31/30 as the 0.3 pt that moving two lines would save stay within one multiple of 16.97 pt, 4 pt would not, docx/local/ps-h*). The columns end below the space after their last paragraph; the next section's first paragraph keeps what its space before has more. Separator lines are 0.75 pt wide in the middle of the gap, from the columns' top to the bottom of the longest column. Columns of different widths (w:equalWidth 0): a paragraph that goes on in another column breaks its rest anew at that column's width, in balanced columns too, the balance trying each height with the text flowing anew (docx/local/uc-1 to uc-8: 90 and 400 words in columns of 132 and 264 pt, either first; docx/external/ libreoffice/extras-ooxmlexport/fdo77812, tdf166553_paraStyleAfterBreak, docx/external/openxml-sdk/complex0).

**Elements (2):** [`w:col`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wcol), [`w:cols`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wcols)

**Specification:** §17.6.3 Single Column Definition (p.567); §17.6.4 Column Definitions (p.568)

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.212, 2.1.213

<a id="section.pageBorders"></a>

### Page borders

`section.pageBorders` · P2 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Line borders and the 165 art borders of the format catalog, measured from the page edge or the text, on all pages, the first page or all but the first.

**Office behaviour:** Line borders as Word prints them: from the page's edge a side's outer edge stands its space from the edge, from the text its inner edge its space outside the margins and around a header and footer the page has (w:bordersDoNotSurroundHeader, w:bordersDoNotSurroundFooter); on all pages, the first or all but the first; in front of or behind the text. Lines and gaps are floored to Word's 0.12 pt device dots; double, triple and thin-thick lines with white gaps (thin lines 0.75 pt, gaps 0.75 pt, half the width or the width) nest at the corners; dotted and dashed lines run between filled corners; embossed and engraved lines in three shades; shadows as black bands of the lines' width (docx/generated/page-borders, docx/local/pgb-a, pgb-b, pgb-c: line styles 85 to 99.9 %). Waves and stroked dash-dots, pictures in Word, are drawn as lines. Art borders: with an art on any side Word draws every side there is with the art and color of the last side at the size of the first art (w:sz in points), nothing when the last side has a line; a square tile in each corner, which ends a side at the page's edge where the side it meets is left out, and between the corners as many tiles as fit of the picture's length in whole device dots plus one, stretched to the side dot by dot (the first tiles a dot shorter). The ten basic art borders (black and white dots, dashes and squares, thin, wide inline, midline and outline lines) are drawn from their measured geometry, the bottom's and right's tiles as Word's own (docx/local/pgb-art2 99.3 %, pgb-art3 99.4 %, sizes 10 to 31 pt). Known deviation: Word's other 155 art borders are pictures of its own (apples, hearts, trees ...) and are left out.

**Elements (8):** [`w:alignBordersAndEdges`](../format/namespaces/w.md#walignbordersandedges), [`w:bordersDoNotSurroundFooter`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wbordersdonotsurroundfooter), [`w:bordersDoNotSurroundHeader`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wbordersdonotsurroundheader), [`w:bottom`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wbottom), [`w:left`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wleft), [`w:pgBorders`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wpgborders), [`w:right`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wright), [`w:top`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wtop)

<a id="section.lineNumbers"></a>

### Line numbers

`section.lineNumbers` · P2 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Numbers in the left margin per line, restarting per page, section or never, every n-th line.

**Office behaviour:** Body lines of numbered sections count, empty ones too, except those of paragraphs with w:suppressLineNumbers, tables, notes, headers and footers; the first line has w:start + 1; the count starts again on every page (newPage, the default), in every section (newSection) or never (continuous); every w:countBy-th number shows, right-aligned w:distance (0.25 in by default) left of its column on the line's baseline, in the Line Number style rather than the paragraph's formatting (docx/local/line-numbers 97.8 %).

<a id="section.pageNumbers"></a>

### Page numbering

`section.pageNumbers` · P0 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** Number format and restart per section, chapter numbers from heading styles; PAGE fields read them.

**Office behaviour:** PAGE shows the page number in the section's format (w:pgNumType/@fmt) unless the field's own \* switch names one; w:pgNumType/@start restarts the count at the section's first page (docx/local/hf-parity). With chapter numbers (w:chapStyle) the number of the last heading of that level on the page or before it, in its level's format alone (Chapter B shows B), stands before it with the separator of w:chapSep (hyphen, period, colon, em dash, en dash; docx/local/chapter: a page ending one chapter and starting the next shows the next's).

<a id="section.docGrid"></a>

### Document grid

`section.docGrid` · P1 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Line pitch (lines snap to the grid) and character pitch, the default of East Asian templates; changes the line height of every paragraph that snaps to the grid.

**Office behaviour:** A line grid (w:docGrid type lines, linesAndChars, snapToChars) makes every line of auto spacing in paragraphs that snap to it as many whole pitches high as its natural height needs, times its spacing factor, the extra half above and half below; exact spacing and w:snapToGrid off keep their heights; headers and footers do not snap (docx/local/docgrid: 18 and 15 pt grids with Arial 10 to 30 pt, every line within 0.13 pt). A character grid adds w:charSpace / 4096 pt to every character (East Asian lines exact, Latin within 0.03 pt a character).

**Elements (1):** [`w:docGrid`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wdocgrid)

**Specification:** §17.6.5 Document Grid (p.570)

<a id="section.printing"></a>

### Printer and book fold settings

`section.printing` · P3 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** Printer trays are ignored; book fold and two pages per sheet change the page geometry and are measured.

**Office behaviour:** Paper trays and printer settings are ignored. Book fold and two pages per sheet already halve the paper in w:pgSz; with w:printTwoOnOne odd pages swap their top and bottom margins, book fold keeps them (docx/local/print-twoonone and print-bookfold: every line as in Word).

<a id="headerFooter.parts"></a>

### Headers and footers

`headerFooter.parts` · P0 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** First, even and default headers per section, inherited from the previous section when missing; laid out by the same engine at w:header/w:footer distance. A header taller than the top margin pushes the body down.

**Office behaviour:** The header starts w:header below the page's top edge with the space before its first paragraph; the footer ends w:footer above the bottom edge with the space after its last. A header reaching below the top margin pushes the body down to its end including that space after, a footer reaching above the bottom margin moves the body's end up (docx/local/hf-tall, hf-short). The first page of a section with a title page has the first header, also the page a continuous section begins at the top of, not the one after a page where it has lines (docx/local/hfc-0t, hfc-2t; a page's header is its top's section's), even page numbers the even header when the settings ask for different odd and even headers; a section without a header of a type inherits the previous section's, and a type no section gives is an empty header. Word's footers stand 0.14 pt lower than the footer distance says (its 600 dpi device grid), which the layout does not copy. Floating tables of headers and footers stand as in the body and take no room in the story; those anchored to its text make it reach as far as they do, the paragraph they are anchored to going below them where they begin above it, the story's other text over them; the body's text goes around those anchored to the page, and a table of the body below one that leaves it less than half its width beside it (docx/external/libreoffice/extras-ooxmlexport/tdf149089, tdf162746, poi/PageSpecificHeadFoot, core-header_footer/tdf79639, writerfilter-dmapper/floating-table-header, core-unocore/tdf108272-1-minimal).

**Elements (6):** [`w:evenAndOddHeaders`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wevenandoddheaders), [`w:footerReference`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wfooterreference), [`w:ftr`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wftr), [`w:hdr`](../format/namespaces/w.md#whdr), [`w:headerReference`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wheaderreference), [`w:titlePg`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wtitlepg)

<a id="note.footnotes"></a>

### Footnotes

`note.footnotes` · P1 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Footnotes go to the bottom of the page of their reference (or below the text), continue on the next page with the continuation separator when they do not fit, and take space from the body (engine.pagination).

**Office behaviour:** Footnotes are numbered by the order of their references in the format, from the start and with the restarts of the section of the reference (w:footnotePr in w:sectPr, else the settings'; decimal by default, counting on through the sections; eachSect starts each section anew, eachPage each page, numbered from a second layout), their marks drawn in the reference style. A line with references needs room for their notes; where the last note does not fit but shows a line, it stays and the note continues on the next page under the separator, before that page's own notes, the page taking no more text (Word prints its continuation separator as the separator; docx/local/fnpages-a, -d). Lines no page break may come between (those of a paragraph keeping its lines together, the first two and the last two under widow control, the last line of a paragraph kept with the next and that one's first) make room only for the notes before them: a keep chain fills the page as without its own notes, which show what fits below the text, the first that does not fit with the lines that do, and go on to the next page, whole notes before its own; such lines whose notes do not show (all but the last whole, the last with a line) go to the next page with them unless they begin their page, as a chain that cannot break (docx/local/fnkeep-*: 31 cases with Word's pages and note lines; known deviation: a chain going on at the top of a page that begins with notes from the page before, which Word leaves to those notes alone). Notes left after the last page of text fill pages of their own. The notes stand at the bottom of the page, ending at the bottom margin, under the separator (144 pt long, its font's strikeout stroke on whole device dots: Aptos 12 pt 4.3 pt above the baseline and 0.6 pt thick, Times New Roman 10 pt 2.5 and 0.5 pt, Calibri 11 pt 2.8 and 0.8 pt; docx/generated/notes, docx/local/fbn-*, docx/external/libreoffice/extras-ooxmlexport/tdf90810short); a section whose own w:footnotePr puts them beneath the text has them follow its last line (the settings' alone do not; docx/local/fnpages-f, -g); pages of several columns spread whole notes into columns of their own width, each column taking notes until it holds its share of their height, under one separator at the left, every column of text ending above them (docx/local/fnpages-h). Notes of table cells need room with their row. Measured with docx/generated/notes and docx/local/fnpages-a to -h: all lines within 0.2 pt but the note marks, whose superscripts stand within half a point (run.decoration). Above notes a paragraph's last line needs its whole height and the paragraph's space after, from Word 2013 on (docx/local/fbn-*, fbm-*, fbo-*: a line of 24.15 pt with 10 pt after fits where 34.15 pt are left above the notes, not 33 pt; without notes 16.1 pt do, as before Word 2013 with them; docx/external/libreoffice/extras-ooxmlexport/tdf90810short).

<a id="note.endnotes"></a>

### Endnotes

`note.endnotes` · P1 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Collected at the end of the section or the document.

**Office behaviour:** Endnotes follow the document's last paragraph under their separator, in the order of their references, or, where the settings put them at the end of sections (w:endnotePr/w:pos sectEnd), follow the text of each section's last paragraph, an empty section-break paragraph taking no line before them, unless the section has w:noEndnote (then the next section takes them). Their marks follow the format and start of the section of the reference, lower roman by default, counting on through the sections (docx/generated/notes, docx/local/fnpages-c, -e).

<a id="table.structure"></a>

### Rows, cells, merged cells and nested tables

`table.structure` · P0 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Grid columns, horizontal spans, vertical merges, rows that skip grid columns, right-to-left tables.

**Office behaviour:** Grid columns, horizontal spans, vertical merges (a merged cell taller than its rows makes the last one higher; where its rows go to different pages its lines, as laid out from the cell's top whatever its alignment, stay with the row they end in, those of rows on the next page go on at the top of its first row, from Word 2013 on a paragraph that widow control keeps together whole, and the merge's last row there grows to hold them: docx/local/vmb*-*, docx/external/poi/bug65649, docx/external/libreoffice/extras-layout/tdf152298; known deviation: Word aligns what stays on the first page within its rows there), rows that skip grid columns (no lines where no cell is), nested tables, right-to-left tables (the first column at the right, the table at the right margin); tables that follow each other directly are joined with one line between, an inside line: the first's last row's cell bottoms or inside line against the second's first row's cell tops or inside line, neither table's own bottom and top lines (docx/local/tbl-geom, tbl-features, tjn-a … tjn-i; docx/external/libreoffice/extras-ooxmlexport/tdf104061_tableSectionColumns). Rows with exceptions (w:tblPrEx) have their own table borders, side by side over the table's; between two rows each cell brings its own row's inside line, resolved by weight with the other's (docx/local/rowex: a row whose inside lines are red and double has them above and below it; docx/external/powertools/RP/ RP033-Table-Prop-Ex-Change). Horizontal merges of old documents (w:hMerge): a cell that continues a merge widens the cell before it, which keeps its content (the continuing cells' does not show) and takes the right border of the last (docx/local/hmerge, 99.8 %). A cell's empty last paragraph after a nested table takes no room, whatever its spacing and mark (docx/local/nestedend-12, -14, -15). What a cell holds is cut at its inside, between its side lines and from below the row's top line to its bottom: paragraph borders past the lines or below a row of exact height do not show (docx/external/ libreoffice/core-layout/para-border-in-cell-clip: Word's clip from the left line's inner edge to the right one's; the cut of text is not modeled).

<a id="table.width"></a>

### Table and column widths (autofit)

`table.width` · P0 · difficulty 5 · done

**Rendering:** Fixed layout uses the grid; autofit layout computes column widths from the content's minimum and maximum widths like Word, which the specification does not describe: measured with generated tables (engine.tableLayout).

**Office behaviour:** Fixed tables use the grid; autofit tables are recomputed as Word does (measured with docx/local/tbl-autofit in Courier New, ten cases within 0.15 pt): each column's minimum (its widest unbreakable content) and maximum (its content unwrapped, at most 22 inches) with the cells' margins, where narrower than half the line beside them that half (docx/local/cellborder), inline pictures with their width; a preferred cell width fixes its column; where the maxima fit the columns take them (stretched in proportion to them for a wider table width), where the minima fit each gets its minimum and a share of the rest in proportion to max - min; where preferred widths do not fit, columns whose contents need more keep their minimum and the others give up what is missing in proportion to how much they prefer over their contents' minimum (docx/local/tblpic table B, tblpic2, overpref-O1 to -O4 within 0.2 pt: cells preferring 221 pt each on 451 pt beside words of 12, 48 and 30 pt take 143.6, 157.1 and 150.3 pt); where cells prefer percentages that fit and only columns whose contents need more make the table too wide, the others give up what is missing in equal shares (docx/local/t65649-table: four columns of 18 to 38 % give up 1.6 pt each to one of 6.5 % whose word needs 6.4 pt more; poi/bug65649); a paragraph's negative left indent shrinks its column's minimum, a negative right one does not (docx/local/negind); where the contents' own minima do not fit either, those shrink in proportion, preferred widths aside (tblpic3 G: pictures of 200, 200 and 100 pt take 180, 180 and 91 pt; docx/external/powertools/WC/ WC023-Table-4-Row-Image-Before keeps three equal columns although each holds a picture wider than a third); a table narrower than its contents' minima grows to them, up to the width available, before they shrink; one that prefers more than is available keeps them whole, however wide it becomes, in modes 14 and 15 alike (docx/local/tblover-14, -15: pictures of 200 and 250 pt make a table preferring 400 pt 450 pt wide, of 300 and 250 pt 468 pt, the width between the margins; 700 pt of pictures in one preferring 500 pt; docx/external/ poi/bug59058: 553.5 pt of spacer pictures in a centered table preferring 552.75 pt); cells preferring more than the table give way to its width (tblover T13 to T15); widths before and after rows do not widen columns. Cells over several columns widen them where they are too narrow together, the narrower spans first: what is missing goes to the columns nothing has given a width yet (in proportion to the grid's widths), else to those without a preferred width of their own in proportion to their widths, else to the span's last column (docx/local/so-1 to so-10, gw-1 to gw-16: a 300 pt cell over a 15 pt column and two of 250 pt together makes them 17.2 and 282.8 pt, wherever it stands; over columns preferring 100 and 50 pt 100 and 200 pt; a cell over all columns of a table of percentages leaves them theirs; rows that contradict each other take the widest; docx/external/libreoffice/extras-ooxmlexport/ wDateValueFormat, fdo78883). Where the minima shrink, a cell over several columns keeps its contents' minimum, its last column taking it from the others (tdf123705: "n/m" stays on one line). Word 2013 tables start their grid at the indent and move it right by half the left border; older compatibility modes put the first cell's text at the indent and let an autofit table as wide as its column end its last cell's text at the column's right edge, the cell's margin and the line's middle beyond it (docx/local/tw-12-*, tw-14-*, tw-15-*: margins of 0.5 and 8.5 pt reach as far past the right margin, in mode 15 nothing; docx/ external/libreoffice/core-ooxml-pass/fdo78904_fileHang: 440.7 pt between 432 pt margins); tables nested in cells stand as from Word 2013 on in every mode, their left line at the outer cell's text, 100 % as wide as that text, tblInd from there, unlike those of text boxes (docx/local/nz-11 to nz-14b; core-text/ floattable-anchor-height, core-text/fly-minimal-wrap). A nested table needs of its outer column its content and the width it prefers itself, not what its cells prefer: wider ones it gives up in the cell (np-1 to np-4, np-7: a nested cell of 400 pt or three of 150 pt in an outer cell preferring 200 pt leave the outer columns theirs; core-layout/floattable-nested-rowspan, tika/testWORD). Known deviations: a nested table of fixed layout Word narrows to its cell (np-5), ours keeps its grid; for a nested table preferring more than the outer table leaves it Word widens the outer table past its own preferred width (np-6). Cells of tables without any table style (the document has no default table style either) have left and right margins of 0.5 pt (docx/local/tblnostyle-12, -14, -15). A percentage width is of the column's width, whatever the indent (docx/local/tpi-1 to tpi-6: 100 % indented -15.9 or 36 pt is 450.8 pt wide, ending as far before or past the right margin, in modes 14 and 15; docx/external/poi/bug65649). A fixed table with a width of its own scales its grid to it, whatever its cells say; a width in points lies between the outer lines' middles (with cell spacing too), a percentage between their outer edges, before Word 2013 between the middles and wider by the outer cells' margins (docx/local/tblwidth-15, -14, tblwidth2-15, -14).

<a id="table.rows"></a>

### Row height, splitting and header rows

`table.rows` · P0 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Exact and minimum row heights; rows split across pages unless w:cantSplit; header rows repeat on every page.

**Office behaviour:** A row is its top line and its content; at-least heights count without the line, exact ones with it. A row's cells all take its largest top and bottom margins; an at-least height lies between them, an exact one holds the top line and margin and the bottom margin comes below it, in every mode (docx/local/rowmar-11 to -15, -12c, -15c: rows of 30 pt at least with margins of 5 and 3 pt and 0.25 pt lines are 38.25 pt apart, rows of exactly 30 pt 33 pt; beside a cell with 5 pt at the top and one with 10 pt at the bottom a cell without margins puts its text 5 pt down; docx/external/poi/60329). A row fits where it and the line that closes the table below it fit (docx/local/tblbottom-*). Rows that do not fit split at a line of each cell, a paragraph's last line fitting with its space after, before Word 2013 with its cell's bottom margin too (rowmar-11, -12, -14: a line ending 5 pt above the cut over a 10 pt margin goes on); header rows, rows of exact height, rows that may not split (w:cantSplit), rows whose at-least height does not fit and rows with a cell whose first line does not fit move on whole (a picture line that fits without its space after too, and from Word 2013 on a paragraph that widow control keeps whole: two lines on each side of the cut or none, docx/local/rs3-*); the rest of a split row ends below its last paragraph's space after, at least its at-least height, and cells aligned to the middle or bottom align again in each part, the first down to its tallest content (tblsplit-*, docx/local/rowsplit-*, rowsplit2-*: 13 cases within 0.1 pt); a row of a nested table goes on whole where its at-least height does not fit, as the table's own rows do (docx/external/libreoffice/writerfilter-dmapper/ floattable-nested: an inner row of 85 pt with one line moves to the next page); content that the cut divides goes on at the top of the rest, whatever its cell's alignment (docx/local/vs-1 to vs-10: of two centered or bottom-aligned lines with one row line on the page the second stands at the next page's top; docx/external/poi/bug65649); a nested row cut in two goes on below its line and its cell's top margin again (docx/local/sr-1 to sr-5: 6.15 pt further down than a row of the table itself; core-text/floattable-anchor-height). Header rows go with the first row to the next page where it does not fit below them (all of it where it may not split, else a line), repeat after every break where the next row fits below them and stay off where it does not, which still pushes what follows the table to the next page; a row that may not split splits at the top of a page that cannot hold it (tblhdr-*). Where the table breaks the line below the page's last row closes it (docx/local/tbl-autofit, tbl-features), and the part on the next page or in the next column begins with the table's top line where no header rows stand above it, after a cut in a row and between rows, also of floating tables (docx/local/cb-1 to cb-7: a 3 pt outer top line over the continued row of a table whose inside lines are 1 pt or none; core-unocore/floattable-split).

**Elements (3):** [`w:cantSplit`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wcantsplit), [`w:tblHeader`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wtblheader), [`w:trHeight`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wtrheight)

**Specification:** §17.4.6 Table Row Cannot Break Across Pages (p.387); §17.4.49 Repeat Table Row on Every New Page (p.443); §17.4.80 Table Row Height (p.484)

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.120, 2.1.180

<a id="table.borders"></a>

### Table and cell borders and shading

`table.borders` · P0 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Conflicting borders of adjacent cells are resolved by the rules of ISO/IEC 29500 17.4.66 (weight, style, color); with cell spacing every cell keeps its own border. Diagonal borders, shading patterns.

**Office behaviour:** Every vertical line is centered on its grid line (moved right by half the table's left border); text starts the larger of the margin and half the line after it, below the whole top line and the top margin (a 3 pt inside line of a table with 0.5 pt outside lines is centered 0.25 pt right of its grid line; docx/local/tbl- geom). Where two cells meet each side brings its own border, else the table's, nothing where it is nil or none, so the other side's line shows and takes its room (docx/local/nilborder-*, rowborder-*: 21 cases within 0.1 pt); the two are resolved by MS-OI29500 17.4.66: weight (eighths of a point times the style number, dotted and dashed 1), style, darker color, a table's thick inside line over a cell's thin own one too; at the table's edge a cell's own border wins over the table's. Double lines take three times their width, triple five; thin-and-thick lines lie top to bottom and left to right on every side, small gaps and thin lines 0.75 pt, medium ones half the width, large gaps the width beside 1.5 pt and 0.75 pt lines (docx/local/tblcompound-24, -12, as page borders); dotted and dashed lines are dashed strokes; diagonal lines; with cell spacing every cell has its own frame, the cells twice the spacing apart, its content aligned vertically as without spacing, a cell merged down framing its rows and the gaps between them and counting in the last of them (docx/external/poi/bug57031: a SAS table's two-line heading over two header rows of one line), and an autofit table its text fills as wide as the page with all frames (docx/external/poi/bib-chernigovka.netdo.ru_download_docs_17459); a part that goes on on the next page begins below its frame's top line and the spacing (docx/local/sb-*: 4.5, 2.9 and 1.5 pt). Shading patterns are mixed to a flat color.

**Elements (13):** [`w:bottom`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wbottom), [`w:end`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wend), [`w:insideH`](../format/namespaces/w.md#winsideh), [`w:insideV`](../format/namespaces/w.md#winsidev), [`w:left`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wleft), [`w:right`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wright), [`w:shd`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wshd), [`w:start`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wstart), [`w:tblBorders`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wtblborders), [`w:tcBorders`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wtcborders), [`w:tl2br`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wtl2br), [`w:top`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wtop), [`w:tr2bl`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wtr2bl)

<a id="table.styles"></a>

### Table styles and conditional formatting

`table.styles` · P0 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Whole table, banded rows and columns, first and last row and column and the four corner cells, applied in Word's order and switched by w:tblLook.

**Office behaviour:** The table style along its basedOn chain under the table's own properties; conditional formats in Word's order (whole table, column bands, row bands, first and last row, first and last column, corner cells), switched by w:tblLook, bands skipping the first and last row and column; a conditional format's borders apply at its region's edges, its inside lines inside the region; the style's paragraph and run properties sit above the document defaults and under the paragraph style (docx/generated/tables); the row properties of the formats of whole rows (w:trPr) under the row's own, so a first-row format's w:tblHeader repeats the first row where w:tblLook asks for first-row formatting (docx/local/hc-1, hc-0; docx/external/libreoffice/ core-ooxml-pass/fdo78904_fileHang). The first-row format covers the first row and the header rows (w:tblHeader) right after it, one region for borders; with it off, bands still skip the header rows from the top; the last-row format applies to the last row unless it is a first row, also to a table's only row, the last column's alike; w:cnfStyle does not count (docx/local/hdrstyle, hdrstyle2 … 5).

**Elements (7):** [`w:cnfStyle`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wcnfstyle), [`w:defaultTableStyle`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wdefaulttablestyle), [`w:tblLook`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wtbllook), [`w:tblStyle`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wtblstyle), [`w:tblStyleColBandSize`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wtblstylecolbandsize), [`w:tblStylePr`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wtblstylepr), [`w:tblStyleRowBandSize`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wtblstylerowbandsize)

**Specification:** §17.3.1.8 Paragraph Conditional Formatting (p.211); §17.4.7 Table Row Conditional Formatting (p.389); §17.4.8 Table Cell Conditional Formatting (p.395); §17.4.54 Table Style Conditional Formatting Settings Exception (p.448); §17.4.55 Table Style Conditional Formatting Settings (p.449); §17.4.62 Referenced Table Style (p.464) and 4 more

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.121, 2.1.122, 2.1.159, 2.1.160, 2.1.165, 2.1.251, 2.1.252, 2.1.253, 2.1.398, 2.1.41

<a id="table.floating"></a>

### Floating tables

`table.floating` · P1 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Positioned like a frame; text wraps around it (engine.floats).

**Office behaviour:** Tables with w:tblpPr take no room in the text: their outer left edge stands w:tblpX from their column, the margin or the page (or aligns by w:tblpXSpec; before Word 2013 their first cell's text stands there, as at an indent: docx/external/libreoffice/core-layout/floattable-compat14, -compat14-body, -compat14-nosplit in modes 14 and 12, extras-ooxmlexport/fdo68607), their top w:tblpY below the next paragraph's top above its space before (docx/local/floatanchor-*), the margin or the page (or aligns by w:tblpYSpec), the margin without w:vertAnchor; one without a vertical position (tblpY 0, no tblpYSpec or inline) stands at its paragraph before Word 2013 whatever its anchor, at its anchor's top from then on (docx/local/ta-*, tb-*; core-layout/floattable-deleted-anchor, writerfilter-dmapper/floattable-header); text wraps around them on both sides, their distances kept free (docx/local/float-tables 91.3 %: a column-aligned table exactly as in Word). The paragraph after the table adds all its space before to the space after the one before it. A table taller than the rest of the page goes on at the top of the next page's body at the same place across, its rows split at a line of each cell as in the text (in Word 2013 and before alike), header rows repeated, the line below the last row of a page closing each part; the paragraph after it begins at the top of its last page (docx/local/floatsplit-*: anchored to the text exactly as in Word; docx/external/ libreoffice/core-layout/floattable-3pages, floattable-rowsplit, fdo68607 over 8 pages); one anchored to the text whose first row does not fit goes to the next page with its paragraph and stands where it would at it, also above the top margin (docx/local/fn-1, fn-2, fn-4; core-layout/floattable-next-leaf-in-section). Before Word 2013 a table anchored to the page or the margin breaks on its first page where it would at the paragraph after it, as many lines down, so past the bottom margin or above it (floatsplit-14p-*, floattable-compat14, docx/ external/libreoffice/core-doc/floating-table-dummy-text); w:doNotBreakWrappedTables keeps tables whole (writerfilter-dmapper/do-not-break-wrapped-tables). Drawings anchored in its cells stand as in other tables (extras-ooxmlexport/fdo68607: a title text box above the table; extras-layout/tdf116256). In a table cell a floating table stands in the cell's text area (tblpX from its left, the margin as the text, the page from the cell's left edge, tblpXSpec right at its right), tblpY below the next paragraph's top or the content's top, not above the cell, and the cell's text after it wraps around it; the cell is as high as it reaches (docx/ local/nf-1 to nf-7, ng-1, ng-2 in modes 12 and 15; core-layout/floattable-del-empty, extras-layout/ tdf170477, writerfilter-dmapper/floattable-nested-layout); without vertAnchor tblpY counts from the content's top, and one without a vertical position not anchored to the text stands in the text as other tables there, the text below it (docx/local/nh-*, ni-* in modes 14 and 15); in notes it stays in the text (writerfilter-dmapper/floattable-footnote-redline). A table with w:tblOverlap never moves off a floating table before it on the page that it would overlap: right of it where it fits in its horizontal anchor's span, both distances kept, else below it, its distance left then counting from its place (docx/local/tc-5, td-*; core-objectpositioning/floattable-tbl-overlap). Known deviations: a table centered on the margin stands about 5 pt further right in Word; of two floating tables after each other in a cell Word sets the second below the first before Word 2013, beside it from then on (docx/local/ng-3 to ng-5), ours at its paragraph; a line centered beside a floating table centers in its segment in Word (nf-4); tables anchored to the page that do not fit, which Word 2013 begins on the next page (docx/local/floatsplit-15p-*); the compatibility setting allowTextAfterFloatingTableBreak (text after the table beside its first part; docx/external/libreoffice/writerfilter-dmapper/floattable-wrap-on-all-pages).

**Elements (2):** [`w:tblOverlap`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wtbloverlap), [`w:tblpPr`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wtblppr)

**Specification:** §17.4.56 Floating Table Allows Other Tables to Overlap (p.450); §17.4.57 Floating Table Positioning (p.452)

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.161, 2.1.162

<a id="table.cells"></a>

### Cell alignment, text direction and header cells

`table.cells` · P1 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Vertical alignment, rotated text in cells, cells whose end mark takes no height.

**Office behaviour:** Vertical alignment; rotated text (tbRl starts at the cell's top right and runs down, btLr at its bottom left and runs up; rotated columns are as wide as their lines; docx/local/tbl-features); tbRlV and tbLrV run down like tbRl with East Asian characters upright, tbLrV's lines from the left, lrTbV keeps lines across with East Asian characters on their side; vertical lines are thicker (paragraph.bidi; docx/local/vertical2, vertical5); w:noWrap keeps a cell unwrapped in autofit; w:hideMark lets an empty cell take no height. Fit text of cells (w:tcFitText) changes nothing in Word's printout: text too long for its cell wraps as in any other cell (docx/local/fittext).

**Elements (5):** [`w:header`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wheader), [`w:headers`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wheaders), [`w:hideMark`](../format/namespaces/w.md#whidemark), [`w:textDirection`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wtextdirection), [`w:vAlign`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wvalign)

**Specification:** §17.3.1.41 Paragraph Text Flow Direction (p.268); §17.4.18 Header Cell Reference (p.411); §17.4.19 Header Cells Associated With Table Cell (p.413); §17.4.21 Ignore End Of Cell Marker In Row Height Calculation (p.415); §17.4.72 Table Cell Text Flow Direction (p.477); §17.4.83 Table Cell Vertical Alignment (p.488) and 2 more

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.131, 2.1.132, 2.1.134, 2.1.172, 2.1.181, 2.1.63

<a id="field.structure"></a>

### Simple and complex fields

`field.structure` · P0 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Show the cached result between the separate and end field characters, nested fields included; which fields Word recomputes when it prints (PAGE, NUMPAGES ...) is measured (field.evaluation).

**Office behaviour:** Field instructions are hidden, results between the separate and end characters show, nested fields included (a field inside another's instruction is part of the instruction). The results of PAGE, NUMPAGES and SECTIONPAGES are computed after pagination (field.evaluation); others keep their saved result.

**Elements (5):** [`w:delInstrText`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wdelinstrtext), [`w:fldChar`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wfldchar), [`w:fldData`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wflddata), [`w:fldSimple`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wfldsimple), [`w:instrText`](../format/namespaces/w.md#winstrtext)

**Specification:** §17.16.13 Deleted Field Code (p.1272); §17.16.18 Complex Field Character (p.1277); §17.16.19 Simple Field (p.1281); §17.16.23 Field Code (p.1289); Part 4 §14.10.5 Custom Field Data (p.196); Part 4 §14.10.6 Custom Field Data (p.197)

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.519

<a id="field.evaluation"></a>

### Field evaluation

`field.evaluation` · P1 · difficulty 4 · done

The 72 field codes of the format catalog (field-codes.md): page and section numbers, references, sequences, dates, document properties, formulas, IF, TOC and index entries.

**Rendering:** Page-dependent fields (PAGE, NUMPAGES, SECTIONPAGES, PAGEREF, STYLEREF in headers) are computed after pagination; others keep their cached result unless Word is measured to update them.

**Office behaviour:** Measured with docx/local/fields, a document whose fields saved wrong results, printed by Word: Word computes PAGE, NUMPAGES, SECTIONPAGES, PAGEREF (the bookmark's page in its section's format; \p above, below or on page n), REF (the bookmark's text, its paragraph's number), STYLEREF (in a header or footer the first paragraph of the style on its page, \l the last, else the last before it; in the body the nearest before the field; a heading level or a built-in heading's name in Word's languages; of a character style the text of the first or last runs in it, docx/external/libreoffice/extras-ooxmlexport/tdf163894_from_top: a dictionary's headers; a header measured for its page's text sees that page's paragraphs of the layout before), SEQ (\c, , \h, \s), SECTION, IF (numbers or text, wildcards), SYMBOL, DATE, TIME and PRINTDATE (when it prints, in the \@ picture or the language's short date and time) and SAVEDATE (when the document was saved), all in the field's language (w:lang), not the computer's: its names, its short date and time and its AM and PM as Word has them, none in most European languages, upper case in English, the Gregorian calendar in all (docx/local/datelang: 18 languages on a German Windows); the page-dependent ones come from a layout before. PAGE, NUMPAGES and SECTIONPAGES of headers and footers carry the number format switches (roman, ROMAN, alphabetic, ALPHABETIC, Arabic, ArabicDash, Ordinal, CardText, OrdText, Hex) and about 60 ST_NumberFormat formats; NUMPAGES counts blank pages (docx/local/hf-parity). Word keeps the saved results of the others and so does the layout: document properties (AUTHOR, TITLE, DOCPROPERTY), formulas, FILENAME, FILESIZE, NUMWORDS, NUMCHARS, USERNAME, DOCVARIABLE, MERGEFIELD, QUOTE, CREATEDATE. Texts Word words in the language of its user interface follow the reader's culture, German as measured with docx/local/fielderrors (REF and PAGEREF to a missing bookmark, STYLEREF without such a paragraph, SEQ without a name, \p "oben", "unten", "Seite n"), else English; Word sets error texts in bold before Word 2013 (fielderrors-14, -12); a field without an instruction shows nothing. A field Word computes shows its result also where the document saved none (no separate character; tdf154478: a PAGEREF of a missing bookmark inside a table of contents shows the error text). Known deviation: LISTNUM keeps its saved result.

**Elements (4):** [`w:docVar`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wdocvar), [`w:docVars`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wdocvars), [`w:listSeparator`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wlistseparator), [`w:updateFields`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wupdatefields)

**Specification:** §17.15.1.31 Single Document Variable (p.1032); §17.15.1.32 Document Variables (p.1033); §17.15.1.56 List Separator for Field Code Evaluation (p.1053); §17.15.1.90 Automatically Recalculate Fields on Open (p.1088)

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.403, 2.1.408, 2.1.424

<a id="field.bookmarks"></a>

### Bookmarks

`field.bookmarks` · P1 · difficulty 1 · done

**Rendering:** HTML anchors; REF and PAGEREF fields and hyperlinks refer to them.

**Office behaviour:** Bookmarks become HTML anchors where their paragraph begins on its page. A bookmark between paragraphs (as before the first paragraph of a content control) starts at the next paragraph and ends after the one before (docx/external/libreoffice/extras-ooxmlexport/tdf154478: PAGEREF finds the table of contents heading).

<a id="field.forms"></a>

### Legacy form fields

`field.forms` · P2 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Text fields show their result, check boxes a box in their size (checked or not), drop-downs the selected entry.

**Office behaviour:** Text inputs show their result, drop-downs their chosen entry (w:result, else w:default). A check box is a character of the run's font at its size (w:size, else the font size): its advance the font's line height, a square 2.28 pt smaller, 1.08 pt in from its left and above its bottom, in 0.48 pt lines, crossed when checked (docx/local/form-boxes: Arial 8 to 36 pt, fixed sizes; docx/generated/form-fields 94.1 %).

<a id="sdt.structure"></a>

### Content controls

`sdt.structure` · P0 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** Block, row, cell and inline content controls render their content; frames and tags are screen-only.

**Office behaviour:** Block, row, cell and inline content controls render their content; their frames, tags and titles are screen- only (docx/generated/content-controls 94.2 %).

<a id="sdt.dataBinding"></a>

### Data binding

`sdt.dataBinding` · P2 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Bound content controls take their text from the custom XML part (XPath); whether Word refreshes the document's text from the part when it opens is measured.

**Office behaviour:** Word refreshes bound controls from their data when it opens a document, and so do we: the XPath of w:dataBinding (with its prefix mappings) selects a node of the custom XML part with the store item id, or of the core or extended properties; text controls show its value, date controls its date in their w:dateFormat and w:lid, list controls the matching entry's display text; a missing node keeps the stored text (docx/local/binding 98.8 %, every line as in Word).

<a id="review.comments"></a>

### Comments

`review.comments` · P2 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Shown next to the pages (not on them) with their ranges highlighted; whether and how Word's PDF export includes comments is measured.

**Office behaviour:** Without markup Word prints nothing of comments, and neither do we by default: ranges, references and the comments part are skipped (docx/generated/revisions). With markup (the converter's markup option, as Word prints with markup: docx/local/markup-a, markup-d, markup-e, markup-f, markup-revisions; 84 to 98 %) a comment's range is shaded in its author's color at lightness 0.92 between thin brackets of the color, and its balloon stands in a gray pane (F2F2F2) 0.1 in right of the text, 3.6 in wide: 23.76 pt into the pane, 232.97 pt wide, as high as its text with 2.3 pt above and 1.8 pt below, in the shade of the range with an outline of 0.48 pt in the author's color, stacked 1.15 pt apart in the order of their places, at their line's top where there is room; a dotted line runs from the range's end along the line's bottom to the pane and on to 6 pt below the balloon's top. The balloon says "Commented [initials and number]:" in bold Segoe UI 9 pt, then the comment's text in its own fonts at 9 pt; a reply (w15:commentEx paraIdParent) has its own balloon labeled with "R" and the number it answers and no shading of its own; a resolved comment (done) is paler (range and balloon at lightness 0.98, outline and line at 0.875, text BFBFBF). Authors take Word's eight colors (D13438, 0078D4, 5C2E91, 498205, CC3595, 7160E8, 038387, 6D5700) in the order they appear (Word goes on counting over the documents of a session). The page with its pane is made smaller to fit the paper Word picks (a standard paper of the page's size within 10 pt, else A4 where the page fits, else Letter), at its top left in the middle of its height. Balloons speak German where the culture is German (measured), else English. Known deviation: the scale differs from Word's by up to 0.1 % (Word's pane width rounds).

**Elements (18):** [`w15:commentEx`](../format/namespaces/w15.md#w15commentex), [`w15:commentsEx`](../format/namespaces/w15.md#w15commentsex), [`w15:people`](../format/namespaces/w15.md#w15people), [`w15:person`](../format/namespaces/w15.md#w15person), [`w15:presenceInfo`](../format/namespaces/w15.md#w15presenceinfo), [`w16cei:commentEntityInfo`](../format/namespaces/w16cei.md#w16ceicommententityinfo), [`w16cei:extLst`](../format/namespaces/w16cei.md#w16ceiextlst), [`w16cex:commentExtensible`](../format/namespaces/w16cex.md#w16cexcommentextensible), [`w16cex:commentsExtensible`](../format/namespaces/w16cex.md#w16cexcommentsextensible), [`w16cex:extLst`](../format/namespaces/w16cex.md#w16cexextlst), [`w16cid:commentId`](../format/namespaces/w16cid.md#w16cidcommentid), [`w16cid:commentsIds`](../format/namespaces/w16cid.md#w16cidcommentsids), [`w:annotationRef`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wannotationref), [`w:comment`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wcomment), [`w:commentRangeEnd`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wcommentrangeend), [`w:commentRangeStart`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wcommentrangestart), [`w:commentReference`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wcommentreference), [`w:comments`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wcomments)

<a id="review.revisions"></a>

### Tracked changes

`review.revisions` · P1 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Render the document with all changes accepted (final view); a markup view with colored insertions, struck deletions and change bars follows Word's simple and all markup views.

**Office behaviour:** Printed as Word exports without markup by default: insertions and moved-to text stay, deletions and moved- from text are left out, deleted table rows (w:trPr/w:del) disappear, a paragraph whose mark is deleted joins the next, which keeps its own formatting and number; formatting changes show the new formatting (docx/generated/revisions 91.8 %). With markup (the converter's markup option, Word's "All Markup" with comments and formatting in balloons; docx/local/markup-a, markup-b, markup-c, markup-revisions) insertions are underlined and deletions struck through in their author's color, moved text dark green (2C6234), struck through twice where it was and underlined twice where it is; deleted rows and paragraph marks stay; a change bar 0.75 pt wide stands half an inch left of the text beside every line with a change, over neighboring changed lines and the space between them; formatting changes of runs, paragraphs and tables get balloons ("Formatted:" with what the properties now have that they did not: font, size, bold, italic, underline, strikethrough, color, caps, position, highlight, hidden, spacing; alignment, indents, spacing, line spacing, keep options; 37 cases word for word as Word's German user interface says them), joined by a dotted line from a small triangle at the run's start or at the text's right edge. Without balloons the page keeps its size. Known deviation: only the all markup view with Word's default balloons; English balloon texts follow Word's terms but were not measured.

<a id="drawing.inline"></a>

### Inline objects

`drawing.inline` · P0 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** An inline object is one box on the line, standing on the baseline, its effect extent added around it.

**Office behaviour:** Pictures, shapes, groups, canvases, charts and SmartArt are converted to PresentationML and drawn by the slide renderer with the document's theme and color mapping. An inline drawing's box (its extent with the effect extents) stands on the baseline as all ascent; the extra of multiple line spacing comes from the fonts (a 90 pt picture beside 12 pt Aptos at 1.16 lines makes the line 90 + 3.38 + 2.32 pt high, a picture alone 160.5 + 2.32 pt; docx/generated/drawings); a picture alone smaller than its paragraph mark's line takes that line's whole height (docx/local/smallpic-single, -115).

**Elements (6):** [`w:drawing`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wdrawing), [`wp:cNvGraphicFramePr`](../format/namespaces/wp.md#wpcnvgraphicframepr), [`wp:docPr`](../format/namespaces/wp.md#wpdocpr), [`wp:effectExtent`](../format/namespaces/wp.md#wpeffectextent), [`wp:extent`](../format/namespaces/wp.md#wpextent), [`wp:inline`](../format/namespaces/wp.md#wpinline)

**Specification:** §17.3.3.9 DrawingML Object (p.342); §20.4.2.4 Common DrawingML Non-Visual Properties (p.3119); §20.4.2.5 Drawing Object Non-Visual Properties (p.3119); §20.4.2.6 Object Extents Including Effects (p.3121); §20.4.2.7 Drawing Object Size (p.3125); §20.4.2.8 Inline DrawingML Object (p.3126)

<a id="drawing.anchor"></a>

### Floating objects and their position

`drawing.anchor` · P0 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Positioned relative to page, margin, column, paragraph, line or character, by offset or alignment, sized relative to the page or margins; behind or in front of text, stacked by relativeHeight, kept inside table cells with layoutInCell.

**Office behaviour:** Floating drawings stand at their offset from, or aligned in, the frame wp:positionH and wp:positionV name, at a percentage of it, or at wp:simplePos; behind or in front of the text by behindDoc and relativeHeight (docx/generated/drawings). The paragraph's top lies above its own space before (below the space after the paragraph before), the line's is that of the line holding the anchor (docx/local/anchor-rules, within 0.05 pt); an anchor in a part of the paragraph in a later column or page counts from that part and its column (extras-layout/shape-anchored-in-follow: a rectangle anchored at the end of a paragraph that goes on in the next page's second column stands there; extras-ooxmlexport/tdf153613_anchoredAfterPgBreak6); relative to the character counts from the start of the character before the anchor (docx/local/anchor- char, within 0.01 pt). In table cells the column and margin across are the cell's text area, the page and its margins the cell inside its line, down the page and margins the cell's content, whether layoutInCell is set or not (docx/local/cell-anchor). Sizes relative to the page or its margins (wp14:sizeRelH, wp14:sizeRelV) apply to shapes as they are and to pictures only both together, scaled evenly to fit (docx/local/size-rel-shape, size-rel). Alignment places the drawing with its effect extent (wp:effectExtent), with or without an effect to fill it, each side where its alignment meets it, across the page and the margins (docx/local/sa-d1, sa-d2, sb-*, sc-*: 80 pt left and 36 pt above take a shape centered at the page's top 40 pt right and 36 pt down), except for shapes sized relative to the page or margins, whose stored extent is not their own (extras-ooxmlimport/groupshape-relsize, DB-GlossaryDocuments/FooterContent-built).

<a id="drawing.wrap"></a>

### Text wrapping around objects

`drawing.wrap` · P0 · difficulty 5 · done

**Rendering:** Square, tight and through wrapping along the wrap polygon, top and bottom, with wrap distances (engine.floats).

**Office behaviour:** Square wrapping keeps the box with the effect extents and the wrap distances free, top and bottom the whole width; tight and through wrapping keep free what of the wrap polygon (21600ths of the extent) lies beside each line's text, from its ascent to its descent, with the left and right distances; text goes to both sides, the left or right one or the larger (wrapText). Objects push every line of their page, also above their anchor (docx/generated/wrapping, docx/generated/wrap-types: all lines within 0.2 pt).

**Elements (8):** [`wp:lineTo`](../format/namespaces/wp.md#wplineto), [`wp:start`](../format/namespaces/wp.md#wpstart), [`wp:wrapNone`](../format/namespaces/wp.md#wpwrapnone), [`wp:wrapPolygon`](../format/namespaces/wp.md#wpwrappolygon), [`wp:wrapSquare`](../format/namespaces/wp.md#wpwrapsquare), [`wp:wrapThrough`](../format/namespaces/wp.md#wpwrapthrough), [`wp:wrapTight`](../format/namespaces/wp.md#wpwraptight), [`wp:wrapTopAndBottom`](../format/namespaces/wp.md#wpwraptopandbottom)

**Specification:** §20.4.2.9 Wrapping Polygon Line End Position (p.3129); §20.4.2.14 Wrapping Polygon Start (p.3134); §20.4.2.15 No Text Wrapping (p.3135); §20.4.2.16 Wrapping Polygon (p.3136); §20.4.2.17 Square Wrapping (p.3137); §20.4.2.18 Through Wrapping (p.3140) and 2 more

<a id="drawing.textBoxes"></a>

### Text boxes

`drawing.textBoxes` · P0 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** The content is WordprocessingML laid out by our engine inside the shape's text rectangle (bodyPr insets, anchoring, rotation, autofit); linked text boxes continue the text from one box to the next.

**Office behaviour:** A shape's text box content is laid out like body text in the shape's rectangle less the insets of wps:bodyPr, measured from the geometry's text rectangle and from the outline's inner edge (half the outline further in: 3 pt with a 6 pt outline), at its top, middle or bottom (docx/generated/textboxes). Vertical text turns every line a quarter, the insets staying with the shape's sides: vert and eaVert run the lines down, the first at the right; vert270 runs them up, the first at the left; mongolianVert and wordArtVert run them down, the first at the left; eaVert, mongolianVert and the WordArt kinds set East Asian characters upright; vertical lines are thicker (paragraph.bidi; docx/local/vertical6). Linked text boxes (wps:txbx id, wps:linkedTxbx id and seq) show the lines of the story that do not fit the boxes before them. A shape that resizes to fit (a:spAutoFit) is as high as its text and insets; a box that does not wrap (wrap none) keeps its lines whole and widens to the longest (docx/local/textboxes: every case as in Word, 96.7 %). Known deviation: linked boxes of different widths keep the first box's line breaks.

**Elements (7):** [`w:textboxTightWrap`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wtextboxtightwrap), [`w:txbxContent`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wtxbxcontent), [`wp:linkedTxbx`](../format/namespaces/wp.md#wplinkedtxbx), [`wp:txbx`](../format/namespaces/wp.md#wptxbx), [`wp:txbxContent`](../format/namespaces/wp.md#wptxbxcontent), [`wps:linkedTxbx`](../format/namespaces/wps.md#wpslinkedtxbx), [`wps:txbx`](../format/namespaces/wps.md#wpstxbx)

**Specification:** §17.3.1.40 Allow Surrounding Paragraphs to Tight Wrap to Text Box Contents (p.267); §20.4.2.34 Textual contents of shape (p.3156); §20.4.2.37 Textual contents of shape (p.3157); §20.4.2.38 Rich Text Box Content Container (p.3157); Part 4 §14.9.1.1 Rich Text Box Content Container (p.177); MS-ODRAWXML §2.13.3.1 CT_LinkedTextboxInformation (p.157) and 1 more

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.1779, 2.1.62

<a id="shape.basic"></a>

### Shapes

`shape.basic` · P0 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** Shared DrawingML renderer, as pptx `shape.basic` (done).

**Office behaviour:** Word drawings reach the renderer shared with PowerPoint as their PresentationML counterparts (wps:wsp as p:sp, pic:pic as p:pic, wpg as p:grpSp, charts and diagrams as p:graphicFrame) with the document's theme and w:clrSchemeMapping; drawn as pptx shape.basic (done). Verified against Word with docx/generated/shapes- effects (99.1 %).

<a id="text.body"></a>

### Text body and text box

`text.body` · P0 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Shared DrawingML renderer, as pptx `text.body` (done).

**Office behaviour:** Word drawings reach the renderer shared with PowerPoint as their PresentationML counterparts (wps:wsp as p:sp, pic:pic as p:pic, wpg as p:grpSp, charts and diagrams as p:graphicFrame) with the document's theme and w:clrSchemeMapping; drawn as pptx text.body (done). In Word documents DrawingML text is that of charts and SmartArt (shape text is WordprocessingML); verified with the chart titles, axis and data labels and legends of docx/generated/charts* and the node text of docx/generated/smartart.

<a id="chart.scatterBubble"></a>

### Scatter and bubble charts

`chart.scatterBubble` · P1 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Shared DrawingML renderer, as pptx `chart.scatterBubble` (done).

**Office behaviour:** Word drawings reach the renderer shared with PowerPoint as their PresentationML counterparts (wps:wsp as p:sp, pic:pic as p:pic, wpg as p:grpSp, charts and diagrams as p:graphicFrame) with the document's theme and w:clrSchemeMapping; drawn as pptx chart.scatterBubble (done). Verified against Word with docx/generated/charts, charts-more, charts-more2 and charts-more3 (88 to 98 %) and docx/local/bubble-* (the default bubble chart with its largest bubble moved to x from 1.8 to 2.95 at bubble scales 50, 100 and 200 %). Known deviation: Word lets a bubble near the end of its x axis grow the axis by a major unit a little earlier than the bubble's radius says, by an amount that grows faster than the bubble (at 100 % a 49.8 pt bubble at 2.58 of an axis to 3 in a 392 pt plot area, at 200 % an 81 pt one at 2.3; at 50 % as here); Word's default bubble chart therefore ends at 3.5 where it ends at 3 here, while PowerPoint's, the same chart in a smaller plot area, ends at 3.5 in both.

**Elements (9):** [`c15:bubble3D`](../format/namespaces/c15.md#c15bubble3d), [`c16:bubble3D`](../format/namespaces/c16.md#c16bubble3d), [`c:bubble3D`](../format/namespaces/c.md#cbubble3d), [`c:bubbleChart`](../format/namespaces/c.md#cbubblechart), [`c:bubbleScale`](../format/namespaces/c.md#cbubblescale), [`c:scatterChart`](../format/namespaces/c.md#cscatterchart), [`c:scatterStyle`](../format/namespaces/c.md#cscatterstyle), [`c:showNegBubbles`](../format/namespaces/c.md#cshownegbubbles), [`c:sizeRepresents`](../format/namespaces/c.md#csizerepresents)

<a id="chart.ex"></a>

### Office 2016 charts (chartex)

`chart.ex` · P2 · difficulty 4 · done

Waterfall, treemap, sunburst, histogram, Pareto, box and whisker, funnel and region map.

**Rendering:** Shared DrawingML renderer, as pptx `chart.ex` (done).

**Office behaviour:** Word drawings reach the renderer shared with PowerPoint as their PresentationML counterparts (wps:wsp as p:sp, pic:pic as p:pic, wpg as p:grpSp, charts and diagrams as p:graphicFrame) with the document's theme and w:clrSchemeMapping. An Office 2016 chart (cx:chart) whose alternate content carries a fallback picture, as Office writes one, shows that picture, Office's own rendering of the chart; without one the chart is drawn as pptx chart.ex (done, measured in PowerPoint). Word's automation refuses to insert Office 2016 charts (AddChart2), so no generated Word document has one.

<a id="math.equations"></a>

### Equations

`math.equations` · P1 · difficulty 4 · done

Inline equations (m:oMath in a paragraph) and display equations (m:oMathPara).

**Rendering:** The OMML layout of pptx `text.math` (done) with Word's paragraph context: display equations take their own lines and justification, inline equations sit on the text baseline, long display equations break at operators.

**Office behaviour:** Equations are laid out by the OMML engine of pptx text.math with the runs' w:rPr: an m:oMath stands in the line on its baseline with its ascent and descent; the display equations of an m:oMathPara take lines of their own, broken before the binary and relation operators of their top level where they are too wide (the next lines indented by m:wrapIndent) and placed by m:jc: centered as a group, centered, left or right (docx/generated/equations, equation by equation within 0.3 pt of Word's lines but the limit with its fraction). A line's height is the equation's ascent and descent and the spacing's extra of the text fonts, the paragraph mark's where the equation stands alone (docx/local/eq-shaded). Word differs from PowerPoint in two rules: delimiters grow to the content's height less the shortfall, not twice its reach from the axis (docx/local/ppt-math: PowerPoint's own probe equations in Word), and a function's name keeps a thin space before an opening delimiter; both draw the hyphen-minus as the minus sign and ∇ and ∂ in italic. Glyphs are drawn as outlines under transparent selectable text.

<a id="math.settings"></a>

### Document math settings

`math.settings` · P1 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Defaults for all equations of the document (math font, break rules, margins, limits placement).

**Office behaviour:** m:mathFont (a font with a MATH table, else Cambria Math), m:defJc, m:wrapIndent, m:wrapRight, m:intLim and m:naryLim apply to all equations of the document. Display equations break at the binary operators of their top level as m:brkBin says (measured with docx/local/mathset-a to -c, every line as in Word): before them (the default), after them, or both (repeat), a repeated minus shown as m:brkBinSub says ("--", "-+", "+-": the sign at the end of the line and the one at the start); an operator at a line's end keeps the space before it. m:lMargin and m:rMargin narrow the room of display equations, also without m:dispDef, which center in it (mathset-d). The equations of one m:oMathPara stand on one line one after the other. m:preSp, m:postSp, m:interSp, m:intraSp and m:smallFrac changed nothing in Word's printout (mathset-d).

**Elements (17):** [`m:brkBin`](../format/namespaces/m.md#mbrkbin), [`m:brkBinSub`](../format/namespaces/m.md#mbrkbinsub), [`m:defJc`](../format/namespaces/m.md#mdefjc), [`m:dispDef`](../format/namespaces/m.md#mdispdef), [`m:intLim`](../format/namespaces/m.md#mintlim), [`m:interSp`](../format/namespaces/m.md#mintersp), [`m:intraSp`](../format/namespaces/m.md#mintrasp), [`m:lMargin`](../format/namespaces/m.md#mlmargin), [`m:mathFont`](../format/namespaces/m.md#mmathfont), [`m:mathPr`](../format/namespaces/m.md#mmathpr), [`m:naryLim`](../format/namespaces/m.md#mnarylim), [`m:postSp`](../format/namespaces/m.md#mpostsp), [`m:preSp`](../format/namespaces/m.md#mpresp), [`m:rMargin`](../format/namespaces/m.md#mrmargin), [`m:smallFrac`](../format/namespaces/m.md#msmallfrac), [`m:wrapIndent`](../format/namespaces/m.md#mwrapindent), [`m:wrapRight`](../format/namespaces/m.md#mwrapright)

<a id="vml.shapes"></a>

### VML shapes, text boxes and WordArt

`vml.shapes` · P1 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** The VML renderer of pptx `legacy.vml` (done), positioned in the text flow by the style attribute (mso-position-*) and w10:wrap; VML text boxes hold WordprocessingML like wps text boxes.

**Office behaviour:** VML shapes (w:pict) are converted to Word drawings: rectangles, rounded rectangles, ovals, lines, preset shapes by o:spt, paths as custom geometries, groups (v:group: the children in its coordorigin and coordsize, nested), pictures (v:imagedata with crop), text boxes (v:textbox with its insets), WordArt (v:textpath), placed by the style attribute (position, margins, mso-position-*, rotation, flip, z-index) and w10:wrap (docx/local/vml-shapes, vml-group). A rounded rectangle's text stands inside its corners, as the DrawingML text rectangle says. An inline shape hangs from the top of its line (of its paragraph's space before on the first line), pictures stand on the baseline; an outlined shape takes the room of its box in whole 0.75 pt pixels, its outline and its shadow's offset right of and below it; spaces between drawings do not make their line taller (docx/local/vml-inline*). Fills and lines as Word draws them (docx/local/vml-fx, vml-angles, vml-pattern): a gradient runs, at the focus 0 Word writes by default, from color2 at the start of its angle (0: the top, 90: the left, 45: the top left corner, the angle applying in the shape stretched to a square) to the fill color, at focus 100 % the other way, at 50 % from the fill color to color2 in the middle and back, at -50 % the other way round, at any other focus the focus applied twice; a negative angle turns it round (the angle plus 180, the focus ±50 % reversed, any other the rest to 100 %); along its colors list where it has one; color2 "fill darken(n)" or "lighten(n)" for one-color gradients; gradientRadial from its focus position outwards; colors blend in linear light along an S-curve, in plain RGB along a straight line where the method is none or linear, and o:opacity2 fades color2 without tinting (within 3 of 255 of Word's pixels); pattern fills as their 8 × 8 bitmap, a point a bit, tiled from the page's corner; double and triple lines (linestyle), line opacity, shadows (v:shadow: an unblurred copy at its offset, 2 pt right and down by default, in its color and opacity, a double shadow's second in color2 at offset2 behind it). Word 97 borders (w10:bordertop, borderleft, borderbottom, borderright) frame the shape outside, width/8 pt each floored to Word's 0.12 pt dots, double lines three times that, dotted and dashed ones dashed (docx/local/vml-border). w10:anchorlock only locks the anchor while editing. A floating shape's shadow widens its extent on its side, which alignment counts (docx/local/sa-v*: a shape centered at the page's top with a shadow 80 pt left and 36 pt up stands 40 pt right and 36 pt down; docx/external/libreoffice/extras-ooxmlexport/testOuterShdw); mso-wrap-distance-* keep wrapped text away (9 pt left and right, none above and below by default); a text box with mso-fit-shape-to-text is as high as its text where it is drawn. Known deviations: a perspective shadow's matrix is left out; the text wraps the stated height of a box fitted to its text.
