<a id="vml.ole"></a>

### Embedded objects and controls

`vml.ole` · P1 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** OLE objects, ActiveX controls and signature lines show their preview picture (often EMF or WMF, see picture.metafiles), as pptx `picture.ole` (done).

**Office behaviour:** Embedded objects (w:object) show the picture of their VML shape like any VML picture; so do ActiveX controls (w:control): Word prints the picture they store, not the control (docx/local/ole-probe: a check box whose stored picture is a red rectangle prints as that rectangle). Signature lines (o:signatureline) Word draws itself whatever picture they store, scaled from 256 × 128 pixels to the shape: on white an X in 32 px Arial 10 px in with its baseline at 68, a line 2 px thick at 75 across the shape, the suggested signer, the signer's title and e-mail in 11 px Segoe UI 10 px in, their baselines at 91, 107 and 123 (docx/local/ole- sigsizes: 192 × 96, 288 × 144 and 120 × 60 pt, with and without signer and title, within 0.2 pt).

**Elements (8):** [`a15:objectPr`](../format/namespaces/a15.md#a15objectpr), [`a15:signatureLine`](../format/namespaces/a15.md#a15signatureline), [`o:OLEObject`](../format/namespaces/o.md#ooleobject), [`w:control`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wcontrol), [`w:movie`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wmovie), [`w:object`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wobject), [`w:objectEmbed`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wobjectembed), [`w:objectLink`](../format/namespaces/w.md#wobjectlink)

**Specification:** §17.3.3.3 Embedded Control (p.337); §17.3.3.17 Embedded Video (p.348); §17.3.3.19 Embedded Object (p.350); §17.3.3.20 Embedded Object Properties (p.351); §17.3.3.21 Linked Object Properties (p.354); Part 4 §14.3.2.1 Floating Embedded Control (p.51) and 3 more

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.103, 2.1.108, 2.1.109, 2.1.110, 2.1.111, 2.1.1756, 2.1.1834

<a id="vml.watermarks"></a>

### Watermarks

`vml.watermarks` · P1 · difficulty 3 · done

Text or picture watermarks are VML shapes in the header, centered on the page behind the text.

**Rendering:** Text watermarks are VML WordArt (v:textpath) scaled to their box; picture watermarks are washed-out pictures.

**Office behaviour:** WordArt (v:textpath) is drawn as text stretched over the shape like a textPlain warp, filled with the shape's fill; VML fits the font's em box to the shape, so the shape shrinks to the capitals' share of it about its turned center (docx/local/vml-watermark, the watermark of a header placed relative to the margins). Picture watermarks are VML pictures.

**Elements (3):** [`v:imagedata`](../format/namespaces/v.md#vimagedata), [`v:shape`](../format/namespaces/v.md#vshape), [`v:textpath`](../format/namespaces/v.md#vtextpath)

**Specification:** Part 4 §19.1.2.11 Image Data (p.443); Part 4 §19.1.2.19 Shape Definition (p.597); Part 4 §19.1.2.23 Text Layout Path (p.677)

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.1800, 2.1.1808, 2.1.1812

<a id="engine"></a>

## Layout engine (cross-cutting)

Features without elements of their own that decide the fidelity of everything else.

| Feature | Priority | Difficulty | Status | Elements | Office notes |
|---|---|---|---|---:|---:|
| [HTML output](#engine.output) | P0 | ●●●○○ | done | 0 | 0 |
| [Pagination](#engine.pagination) | P0 | ●●●●● | done | 0 | 0 |
| [Line layout](#engine.lineLayout) | P0 | ●●●●● | done | 0 | 0 |
| [Floating objects and text wrapping](#engine.floats) | P0 | ●●●●● | done | 0 | 0 |
| [Table layout](#engine.tableLayout) | P0 | ●●●●● | done | 0 | 0 |
| [Font resolution and fallback](#engine.fonts) | P0 | ●●●●○ | done | 0 | 0 |
| [Fidelity measurement](#engine.fidelity) | P0 | ●●●○○ | done | 0 | 0 |

<a id="engine.output"></a>

### HTML output

`engine.output` · P0 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** One self-contained HTML file: fixed-size page boxes with absolutely positioned glyph runs, SVG for drawings, embedded fonts and pictures; print CSS keeps one page per sheet.

**Office behaviour:** One self-contained HTML file: a fixed-size section per page with a named @page per paper size (printed, every page keeps its size), text as SVG text at our positions in the fonts we measured with (fonts the browser cannot find by name, such as Office cloud fonts, embedded), drawings, charts, SmartArt and equations as SVG, pictures embedded, bookmarks as anchors and hyperlinks as links.

<a id="engine.pagination"></a>

### Pagination

`engine.pagination` · P0 · difficulty 5 · done

**Rendering:** Page breaking with widow and orphan control, keep with next, keep lines together, page and column breaks, table rows that split or repeat, footnotes that share the page with their references, section breaks of all types and continuous sections with balanced columns.

**Office behaviour:** Pages, columns, sections, headers and footers and the paragraph keep rules match Word's line positions within 0.1 pt on the generated documents (word-lines); tables break between rows and split rows at lines, repeat their header rows, keep rows that may not split and rows whose paragraphs keep with the next (docx/local/tblpages: 229 lines, mean 0.06 pt); footnotes share the page with their references and continue on the next (note.footnotes); floating objects stay on their anchor's page (engine.floats).

<a id="engine.lineLayout"></a>

### Line layout

`engine.lineLayout` · P0 · difficulty 5 · done

**Rendering:** The text engine of pptx `engine.textLayout` (done) extended by Word's rules: line height from the font's metrics and line spacing, tabs, justification, East Asian grid and kinsoku, hyphenation, inline objects, lines shortened by floating objects.

**Office behaviour:** Lines break at UAX #14 opportunities with Word's kinsoku, and at every space before a character that is not East Asian, also before periods, closing brackets and quotes, slashes and percent signs (docx/local/ spacebreak: 19 characters), after every hyphen and en dash whatever stands before it, digits, punctuation or a space, after the hyphen U+2010 from Word 2013 on, not after slashes, plus signs, underscores or non-breaking hyphens (docx/local/hyphbreak-15, -12: 19 cases), and before and after inline pictures as beside ideographs (docx/local/tblpic3); a word longer than the line breaks between characters, on the line where it begins if nothing but a tab or a list number stands before it there, with at least one character after a number, none needed after a tab (docx/local/lw-*, lx-*, ly-*, lt-*: modes 14 and 15); a tab goes to the next line with the word after it where that word does not fit (lw-6 to lw-10, ly-1, ly-4; docx/external/ libreoffice/extras-ooxmlexport/tdf123054, tdf142404_tabOverMarginC15); trailing spaces hang past the end; spaces never make a line taller or lower, whatever their size or place, and a line of only spaces is the paragraph mark's (docx/local/ts-*, tu-*, tv-*: a 45 pt space before, between or after 9 pt words leaves the line 9 pt in modes 12 to 15, a 45 pt "x" or no-break space does not); justified lines squeeze spaces for one more word where that changes them less than stretching would (paragraph.basic); automatic hyphenation breaks words as Word does (paragraph.hyphenation); line grids snap lines to whole pitches (section.docGrid); East Asian spacing and alignment (paragraph.eastAsian). Glyphs are drawn as browser text at positions from our layout; Word's PDF and ours go through the same rasterizer, so equal positions give equal pixels. Punctuation compression changed nothing in Word's printout (docx/local/ec-*).

<a id="engine.floats"></a>

### Floating objects and text wrapping

`engine.floats` · P0 · difficulty 5 · done

**Rendering:** Anchored objects, frames and floating tables are placed first, then lines are broken into the space around them; objects anchored in later text may move earlier lines, so layout iterates until stable.

**Office behaviour:** Floating drawings are placed on their anchor's page and the page is laid out again around them until it is stable (at most four runs); lines are broken into the segments beside the objects, words that do not fit a segment continue in the next one, lines with no room move down below the object (docx/generated/wrapping, wrap-types), below the first of the objects beside them to end (td-1), beside objects wrapped along their polygons a line at a time, so on the paragraph's line pitch (ub-*), and to the next page where they do not fit below it. A segment takes text from 18 pt, also an empty line's, the first of a line only where the line's first word fits (docx/local/tf-*, tk-*, tq-*, te-*, tg-*, th-*, tl-*: 17.3 pt stay empty, 19.3 take "a an", a word of 40.6 pt goes below an object leaving 39.3 pt); before Word 2013 a line beside objects wrapped square stays where the column has 18 pt beside them, also only outside its indents, takes what of it they leave, a character a line where none, a word breaking where it does not fit (tx-*, ty-*, tz-*, ua-*; powertools/RP/RP001-Tracked-Revisions-01-Accepted), and beside objects wrapped along their polygons a segment takes text from 9 pt (tv-*, tw-*; core-text/fly-minimal-wrap). A line moved down for the height of its paragraph mark stands where it fits with its own (uitest/tdf124675). A paragraph whose own object leaves it no room on the page moves on with the object, and the page it left is laid out without it (docx/local/anchorpush-*). An object kept free above and below that reaches down to its paragraph's place pushes the lines already in the column that it overlaps below it; where its paragraph would then no longer begin in the column, or where the object ends above the paragraph, it takes the paragraph to the next (uc-1 to uc-5, ud-*; generated/wrapping, local/compat11-wrapping; extras-layout/fdo43573-2-min). Objects beside the column leave its lines alone, also those kept free above and below, and a paragraph going on in another column or page goes on beside the objects there (fdo43573-2-min: an image at the top of a page's left column, the right column's text and the last line of a paragraph from the page before). Text frames and drop caps (paragraph.frames) and floating tables (table.floating) take part in the same exclusions. A table does not stand beside the floating objects and frames before it: it begins below every one its part on the page would overlap, from Word 2013 on whatever their wrapping, before only those kept free above and below (docx/local/fx-1 to fx-13, ft-1 to ft-8: below a 40 pt shape wrapped square or top and bottom, also one beside a 70-row table's last rows on the page, and below frames not beside text, anchored to the text or the page; a shape right of the table and one without wrapping leave it; docx/external/tika/testWORD_tableInRunInsideParagraph, docx/external/libreoffice/core-ooxml-pass/ fdo78904_fileHang); below a floating table of the text only where it does not fit beside it and reaches its box, its distances aside (ib-1 to ib-7: a table of 90 pt beside one of 350 pt, of 468 pt below it; writerfilter-dmapper/floattable-then-table, extras-layout/tdf167526; extras-ooxmlexport/tdf92157: a table into the distance of a floating table in the right margin stays). A floating table that may not overlap others (w:tblOverlap never) moves right of the one before it that it would overlap where it fits, else below it (table.floating). Known deviations: a table that fits beside a floating table Word moves beside it (ib-1, ib-2), ours stays at its place across it; in mode 14 Word sets a table beside a shape wrapped square, indented and narrower (fx-5), ours stays over it; a table whose cells' paragraphs have frame properties stands in that frame at its position (lb-1 to lb-4, tdf104394_lostTextbox), ours in the text.

<a id="engine.tableLayout"></a>

### Table layout

`engine.tableLayout` · P0 · difficulty 5 · done

**Rendering:** Word's autofit algorithm, measured against generated tables, and rows that span pages.

**Office behaviour:** Word's autofit: each column's minimum and maximum width with margins (the maximum capped at 1584 pt); preferred widths fixed, giving way where they do not fit; the maxima when they fit, else the minima and the rest in proportion to max - min, else the content minima shrunk (table.width); fixed layout by the grid; rows that split across pages unless cantSplit, repeated header rows, nested tables (docx/local/tbl-autofit 92/92 lines, docx/generated/tables 44/44).

<a id="engine.fonts"></a>

### Font resolution and fallback

`engine.fonts` · P0 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** As pptx `engine.fonts` (done) with Word's font substitution (font.table).

**Office behaviour:** Installed, embedded and Office cloud fonts (Aptos) as in pptx, with Word's own substitution of missing families (font.table) and theme fonts (font.theme). A character the run's font lacks is measured and drawn in the font Word falls back to, by range: emoji Segoe UI Emoji (also before U+FE0F); symbols, dingbats, arrows and Braille Segoe UI Symbol; letterlike and mathematical symbols, technical symbols and enclosed numbers Cambria Math (enclosed letters then MS Gothic or MS Mincho); geometric shapes Arial; Thai, Lao and Khmer Leelawadee UI; Indic scripts Nirmala UI; Georgian Sylfaen; Ethiopic Ebrima; Cherokee Gadugi; Syriac Segoe UI Historic; Tibetan Microsoft Himalaya; Myanmar Myanmar Text; Hangul Malgun Gothic or Batang beside serif and East Asian fonts; Chinese characters and kana MS Mincho, then PMingLiU or Microsoft JhengHei. Lines grow with the fallback's metrics as in Word; spaces and format characters stay in the run's font, marks with their base (docx/local/fallback-arial 96.1 %, fallback-calibri 95.7 %, fallback-times 97 %, every line within 0.1 pt). Color emoji are Chrome's (COLRv1 shading) where Word draws them flat.

<a id="engine.fidelity"></a>

### Fidelity measurement

`engine.fidelity` · P0 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Word (COM) exports every reference document to PDF; its pages are rasterized and compared with our pages like the PowerPoint references, on documents Word generates by COM and on external test documents.

**Office behaviour:** Word (COM) exports every reference document to PDF; Chrome prints our HTML to PDF and the same rasterizer (Windows.Data.Pdf) renders both, page by page at 96 dpi, for the pixel and SSIM scores; pdf-text and word- lines compare Word's PDF layout with ours line by line and glyph by glyph. 29 documents generated by Word through COM (docx/generated, 92.6 % overall) and local probes for each measured rule. The rasterizer ignores the soft masks Chrome writes (reflections, soft edges), which MuPDF shows.

<a id="unassigned-elements"></a>

## Unassigned elements

None: every element that can occur in a DOCX file belongs to at least one feature.
